\mathcal{Z}({\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{t}\ell_{\iota})\leqslant\frac{\mathcal{Z}(\widehat{{\mathcal{F}}}_{t}^{n}\ell_{\iota})}{\mu_{\ell_{\iota}}({\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{n})}\leqslant\frac{\mathbb{B}_{2}}{1-C_{n}t}\leqslant 2\mathbb{B}_{2}\ll B_{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\mu_{\ell_{\iota}}({\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{n})\geqslant\frac{1}{2}.

Therefore, by (
6.31
) and choosing possibly a smaller
p_{c}
one obtains that the boundary of
{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,u}^{\prime}
is only slightly bigger than
2\mathcal{Z}({\mathcal{L}}_{t}^{n}\ell_{\iota})
and so
{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,u}^{\prime}
is a regular standard family. This concludes the proof for the case of two standard pairs. In the general case of two regular standard families
{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}
, by Corollary (
6.15
) point (c), after
N_{*}\sim\ln B_{2}
iterations
\mathcal{Z}({\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{N_{*}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota})\leqslant\mathbb{B}_{2}
. Then, calling
{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{N_{*}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}=\{(p_{\iota,k},W_{\iota,k},\rho_{\iota,k})\}
,

\sum_{p_{\iota,k}:|W_{\iota,k}|\geqslant 1/B_{2}}p_{\iota,k}\geqslant 1-\frac{\mathbb{B}_{2}}{B_{2}}\geqslant\frac{1}{2}.

Here, we have used Lemma
6.12
in the first inequality and Equation (
6.18
) in the second. Therefore, at least half of the mass belongs to regular standard pairs. Calling
{\mathcal{H}}_{\iota}
the standard families formed by these regular standard pairs, we can then apply the argument as above to couple each element of
{\mathcal{H}}_{1}
with any other of the family
{\mathcal{H}}_{2}
. The above argument yields the estimates
N_{*}+N
in place of
N
for the minimum time needed for coupling and
p_{c}/4
in place of
p_{c}
for the coupled mass. This concludes the proof of the Lemma.
∎

6.5.
Decoupling because of discontinuities

In this section we define fake stable foliations for coupling and compute how much mass gets ‘decoupled’ because of discontinuities.

A problematic aspect of Definition
6.18
is that the
\eta
-coupled mass is not guaranteed to be coupled after just some iterations of the map. This happens because we couple mass along vertical lines instead of stable manifolds. However, we find that the mass lost in the process is very small, proportional to some power of the distance between the standard pairs
\eta
. For any
x\in{\mathcal{M}}
and
n\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}
, recall that
{\mathcal{F}}^{n}(x)=(r_{n},\varphi_{n})
and let
{\mathcal{H}}_{n}(x)
be the maximal connected component of

(6.32)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:holonomy-def}{\mathcal{F}}^{-n}\biggl(\biggl\{(r_{n},\varphi)\in{\mathcal{M}}:\varphi\in\biggl[-\frac{\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}\biggr]\biggr\}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{-n,t}^{{\mathbb{H}}}\biggr)

containing
x
. (The above could be empty).
{\mathcal{H}}_{n}(x)
is an approximation of the stable manifold containing
x
and we call
\{{\mathcal{H}}_{n}(x)\}_{x\in{\mathcal{M}}}

fake stable foliation
. For two
\eta
-stacked unstable curves
W_{\iota}
,
\iota\in\{1,2\}
,
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, set

(6.33)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:domain-holonomy}\widehat{W}_{\iota}^{(n)}=\{x\in W_{\iota}:{\mathcal{H}}_{n}(x)\cap W_{3-\iota}\neq\emptyset\},

and we define the
fake holonomy map

\mathbb{h}_{n}:\widehat{W}_{\iota}^{(n)}\to\widehat{W}_{3-\iota}^{(n)}
as

\mathbb{h}_{n}(x)={\mathcal{H}}_{n}(x)\cap W_{3-\iota}.

The map
\mathbb{h}_{n}
is well defined since, by transversality between stable and unstable curves, the intersection in (
6.33
) is unique.
For
x\in\widehat{W}_{1}^{(n)}
, call
H_{x}\subseteq{\mathcal{H}}_{n}(x)
the stable curve connecting
x
to
\mathbb{h}_{n}(x)
. Since the vertical direction belongs to the large stable cone
{\mathcal{C}}^{s}
we have that
{\mathcal{F}}^{k}(H_{x})
is aligned with
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}^{s}
for each
k\in\{0,...,n-1\}
. Moreover, by (
3.8
), for some
C>0
,
\Lambda_{0}>1
and any
k\in\{0,...,n\}
,

(6.34)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:contraction-fake-stable}\begin{split}&|{\mathcal{F}}^{-k}({\mathcal{F}}^{k}(H_{x}))|\geqslant C\Lambda_{0}^{k}|{\mathcal{F}}^{k}(H_{x})|\quad\text{or}\quad|{\mathcal{F}}^{k}(H_{x})|\leqslant C\Lambda_{0}^{-k}|H_{x}|.\end{split}

Note also that
{\mathcal{F}}^{k}(H_{x})
is
k
-homogeneous by construction. Consider two
\eta
-stacked curves
W_{\iota}
. The set
\widehat{W}_{\iota}^{(n)}
where
\mathbb{h}_{n}
is defined consists of many, possibly infinite, connected subsets
\widehat{W}_{\iota,p}^{(n)}
of
W_{\iota}^{(n)}
. The gaps between these domains correspond to points where the fake stable foliation meets a singularity or, near the boundary, where it ‘misses’ the other unstable curve. Fix some
\chi\in(0,1/2)
that represents the contraction among stacked graphs.

Lemma 6.24
.

For any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
big enough and
\widehat{W}_{\iota,p}^{(n)}\subseteq\widehat{W}_{\iota}^{(n)}
with
\mathbb{h}_{n}(\widehat{W}_{1,p}^{(n)})=\widehat{W}_{2,p}^{(n)}
, the two unstable curves
{\mathcal{F}}^{n}(\widehat{W}_{\iota,p}^{(n)})
are
\chi\eta
-stacked.

Proof.

For
x=(r,\varphi)\in\widehat{W}_{1,p}^{(n)}
, recall the notation
H_{x}\subseteq{\mathcal{H}}_{n}(x)
for stable curve connecting
x
to
\mathbb{h}_{n}(x)
. By the definition (
6.32
) of fake holonomy,
{\mathcal{F}}^{n}(H_{x})
are vertical lines, the curves
{\mathcal{F}}^{n}(\widehat{W}_{\iota,p}^{(n)})
are graphs of two
{\mathcal{C}}^{2}
functions
\varphi_{{\mathcal{F}}^{n}(\widehat{W}_{\iota,p}^{(n)})}
defined on the same domain and
|\varphi_{{\mathcal{F}}^{n}(\widehat{W}_{1,p}^{(n)})}(r_{n})-\varphi_{{\mathcal{F}}^{n}(\widehat{W}_{2,p}^{(n)})}(r_{n})|=|{\mathcal{F}}^{n}(H_{x})|
. Since
{\mathcal{H}}_{n}(x)
is aligned with
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}^{s}
and
W_{\iota}
are
\eta
-stacked one has that
|H_{x}|\leqslant C_{\mathrm{cone}}\eta
. Therefore, by (
6.34
), the
{\mathcal{C}}^{0}
distance between
\varphi_{{\mathcal{F}}^{n}(\widehat{W}_{1,p}^{(n)})}
and
\varphi_{{\mathcal{F}}^{n}(\widehat{W}_{2,p}^{(n)})}
is not greater than

\sup_{x\in\widehat{W}_{1,p}^{(n)}}|{\mathcal{F}}^{n}(H_{x})|\leqslant\sup_{x\in\widehat{W}_{1,p}^{(n)}}C\Lambda_{0}^{-n}|H_{x}|\leqslant C\Lambda_{0}^{-n}C_{\mathrm{cone}}\eta\leqslant\chi\eta,

where in the last inequality we consider
n
big enough. For the bound on the
{\mathcal{C}}^{0}
distance of the derivatives, consider any
x=(r,\varphi)\in\widehat{W}_{1,p}^{(n)}
. Note that
\delta:=|x-\mathbb{h}_{n}(x)|\leqslant|H_{x}|\leqslant C_{\mathrm{cone}}\eta
and, setting
\mathbb{h}_{n}(x)=(\overline{r},\overline{\varphi})
, we have that

\begin{split}\gamma:=|\varphi_{W_{1}}^{\prime}(r)-\varphi_{W_{2}}^{\prime}(\overline{r})|&\leqslant|\varphi_{W_{1}}^{\prime}(r)-\varphi_{W_{2}}^{\prime}(r)|+|\varphi_{W_{2}}^{\prime}(r)-\varphi_{W_{2}}^{\prime}(\overline{r})|\\
&\leqslant|\varphi_{W_{1}}^{\prime}(r)-\varphi_{W_{2}}^{\prime}(r)|+D_{2}\delta.\end{split}

Here
D_{2}
is the bound on the second derivatives of standard curves. By the equation above and using the definition of
\eta
-stacked curves we have that
\gamma\leqslant(1+D_{2}C_{\mathrm{cone}})\eta
. We need to bound

\gamma_{n}:=|\varphi_{{\mathcal{F}}^{n}(\widehat{W}_{1,p}^{(n)})}^{\prime}(r_{n})-\varphi_{{\mathcal{F}}^{n}(\widehat{W}_{1,p}^{(n)})}^{\prime}(r_{n})|.

This is the angle between
{\mathcal{F}}^{n}(W_{1})
and
{\mathcal{F}}^{n}(W_{2})
at
{\mathcal{F}}^{n}(x)
and
{\mathcal{F}}^{n}(\mathbb{h}_{n}(x))
. By Lemma
6.25
below,
\gamma_{n}\leqslant C(\delta n/\Lambda^{n}+\gamma/\Lambda^{n})
for some
C>0
and
\Lambda>1
. Hence, the statement follows from the previous estimates for
\delta
and
\gamma
and by taking
n
big enough.
∎

Lemma 6.25
.

[
21
, Exercise 5.44]

Let
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
x\in\widehat{W}_{1}^{(n)}
and
\mathbb{h}_{n}(x)\in\widehat{W}_{2}^{(n)}
. Denote by
\gamma
the angle between the curves
W_{1}
and
W_{2}
at the points
x
and
\mathbb{h}_{n}(x)
and by
\delta=|x-\mathbb{h}_{n}(x)|
. Finally, for
k\in\{1,..,n\}
, denote by
\gamma_{k}
the angle between
{\mathcal{F}}^{k}(W_{1})
and
{\mathcal{F}}^{k}(W_{2})
at
{\mathcal{F}}^{k}(x)
and
{\mathcal{F}}^{k}(\mathbb{h}_{n}(x))
. Then, there exists
C>0
such that
\gamma_{k}\leqslant C(\delta k/\Lambda^{k}+\gamma/\Lambda^{k})
.

In the original reference, Lemma
6.25
is stated for two points joined by a real holonomy and the statement holds for arbitrary
n
. The proof uses the fact that
{\mathcal{H}}_{n}(x)
is aligned with the small stable cone and so it is not affected by the fact that we consider fake stable holonomies. The next result is intuitive: if the fake holonomy map
\mathbb{h}_{n}
is not defined on some point
x\in W_{\iota}
there can be only be two reasons: either
x
is near the boundary of
W_{\iota}
, or the fake stable foliation
{\mathcal{H}}_{n}(x)
intersects a discontinuity. In the second case
x
is close to
W_{\iota}\cap{\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathbb{H}}}_{n,t}
.

Lemma 6.26
.

There exist
C_{\mathrm{cone}}>0
such that, for any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
x\in W_{\iota}\setminus\widehat{W}_{\iota}
, either
x\in[\partial W_{\iota}]_{C_{\mathrm{cone}}\eta}
, or
x\in B(x_{*},C_{\mathrm{cone}}\eta)
for some
x_{*}\in W_{\iota}\cap{\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathbb{H}}}_{n,t}
(or both).

Proof.

Call the two cases (a) and (b). We assume without loss of generality that
x\notin{\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathbb{H}}}_{n,t}
(otherwise we are in case (b) with
x_{*}=x
). Therefore,
{\mathcal{H}}_{n}(x)\neq\emptyset
and both endpoints of
{\mathcal{H}}_{n}(x)
belong to
{\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathbb{H}}}_{n,t}
. Since
W_{\iota}
are
\eta
-stacked, there exists
C_{\mathrm{cone}}>0
such that any long enough stable curve intersecting
W_{\iota}\setminus[\partial W_{\iota}]_{C_{\mathrm{cone}}\eta}
must intersect also
W_{3-\iota}
. Let us assume that we are not in case (a). By the sentence above, it must be that
{\mathcal{H}}(x)
terminates on a point
\overline{x}\in{\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathbb{H}}}_{n,t}
before intersecting the other curve. By Lemma
3.3
, there exists a curve
S\subseteq{\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathbb{H}}}_{n,t}
connecting
\overline{x}
to
\partial{\mathcal{M}}
. Moreover,
S
is aligned to the stable cone and so it is transversal to
W_{\iota}
. By possibly choosing a even bigger
C_{\mathrm{cone}}
in point (a), we can ensure that
\overline{x}
does not belong to a
C_{\mathrm{cone}}^{\prime}\eta
neighbor of
\partial W_{\iota}
for
C_{\mathrm{cone}}^{\prime}>0
sufficiently big. Hence,
S
must intersect
W_{\iota}
in a point
x_{*}
. Finally, for some other
C_{\mathrm{cone}}^{\prime\prime}>0
,
|x-x_{*}|\leqslant|x-\overline{x}|+|\overline{x}-x_{*}|\leqslant 2C_{\mathrm{cone}}^{\prime\prime}\eta
. After renaming the constants, this concludes the proof.
∎

The next Lemma tells that, for any fixed iterate, the complexity is uniform in the parameter of the perturbation.

Lemma 6.27
.

For any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, there exists
K_{n}\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that for any unstable curve
W
(even longer than
\delta_{*}
) and
t\in[0,t_{0}]
,

\#\{W\cap{\mathcal{S}}_{n,t}\}\leqslant K_{n}.

Proof.

For any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
{\mathcal{S}}_{n,t}={\mathcal{S}}_{n}\cup\Xi_{n,t}
is a finite union of
{\mathcal{C}}^{1}
curves. We say that a family
\gamma_{t}:[0,1]\to{\mathcal{M}}
of curves varies continuously with
t
if
t\to\gamma_{t}
is a continuous function w.r.t.​ the
{\mathcal{C}}^{0}
topology on the space of curves. We have that

{\mathcal{S}}_{n}\cup\Xi_{n,t}={\mathcal{S}}_{n}\cup\bigcup_{k=0}^{n}{\mathcal{F}}^{-k}(\partial{\mathcal{H}}_{t}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{-k}).

By transversality,
\partial{\mathcal{H}}_{t}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{-k}
is a finite union of curves which varies continuously with
t
(admitting the presence of degenerate elements, curves are allowed to gradually ‘grow or shrink’ to a point) and by continuity of
{\mathcal{F}}^{-k}
on
{\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{-k}
also
{\mathcal{S}}_{n,t}={\mathcal{S}}_{n}\cup\Xi_{n,t}
is a finite union of curves which vary continuously with
t
. Therefore, by the established continuity, there is a uniform lower bound on the distance between curves in
{\mathcal{S}}_{n,t}
that never intersect for
t\in[0,t_{0}]
. I.e., if two
{\mathcal{C}}^{1}
curves never touch then they are at least
\delta_{**}
apart, for some
\delta_{**}>0
, uniformly in
t
. Hence, any unstable curve with length
\delta_{**}/2
can intersect
{\mathcal{S}}_{n,t}
in at most
M_{n}
points where
M_{n}
is the maximal number of intersections in a point for curves in
{\mathcal{S}}_{n,t}
over all
t\in[0,t_{0}]
. By Lemma
3.4
, there are only finitely many smooth components in
{\mathcal{S}}_{n,t}
uniformly in
t
, so that
M_{n}<\infty
. Then, we divide each unstable curves in
\delta_{**}/2
- long components and since any unstable curve (possibly longer than
\delta_{*}
) is smaller than some constant
\widetilde{C}>0
, related to the diameter of the table, we prove the statement with
K_{n}=M_{n}2\widetilde{C}/\delta_{**}
.
∎

Let
W
be an unstable curve. If
{\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathbb{H}}}_{n,t}\cap W\neq\emptyset
, there are typically accumulation points of
{\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathbb{H}}}_{n,t}
on
W
. In the next Lemma we characterize how fast these discontinuities accumulate around points in
{\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathbb{H}}}_{n,t}\cap W
. We define the enlargement
W_{e}
as an unstable curve that contains
W
so that
2\delta_{*}\leqslant|W_{e}|\leqslant 3\delta_{*}
and
W
belongs to the ‘center’ of
W_{e}
. If the extension of
W
intersects the boundary
\partial{\mathcal{M}}
we do not extend
W_{e}
any further independently of the length of
W_{e}
. We obtain
W_{e}
by extending the function
\varphi_{W}
to a bigger interval in a way that it belongs to
\mathcal{W}
except for the requirement of the length being less than
\delta_{*}
(this is possible thanks to the standard extension theorems, while the actual way we extend
\varphi_{W}
does not play any role in what follows). In practice, these extensions are a tool to book-keep the intersections of unstable curves with
{\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathbb{H}}}_{n,t}
in terms of intersections with
{\mathcal{S}}_{n,t}
. In the next Lemma we fix
k_{0}
from (
3.1
) large enough.

Lemma 6.28
.

There exists
C>0
with the following property. For any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, there exists
k_{0}\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that, for any
k\geqslant k_{0}
,
W\in\mathcal{W}
and
x\in W\cap\cup_{s=0}^{n}{\mathcal{F}}^{-s}(\mathbb{S}_{k})
, there exists
z\in W_{e}\cap{\mathcal{S}}_{n}
with
|x-z|\leqslant Ck^{-2}
.

Proof.

For each
s\in\{0,..,n\}
, there exists
k_{0}\in{\mathbb{N}}
big enough such that if
W\cap{\mathcal{F}}^{-s}(\mathbb{S}_{k})
for some
k\geqslant k_{0}
is non empty then also
W_{e}\cap{\mathcal{S}}_{s}=W_{e}\cap{\mathcal{F}}^{-s}({\mathcal{S}}_{-s})
is non empty. This follows from the fact that
W_{e}
is at least twice as long as
W
, the fact that sending
k_{0}
to infinity
\mathbb{S}_{k}
approaches
{\mathcal{S}}_{0}
and by continuity of
{\mathcal{F}}^{s}
on
{\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{s}
. Fix
k_{0}
to be the largest value so obtained for all
s\in\{0,..,n\}
. Fix some
s\in\{0,...,n\}
and consider the curve
\gamma\subseteq W_{e}
that connects
x\in W\cap{\mathcal{F}}^{-s}(\mathbb{S}_{k})
to the first point
z\in W_{e}\cap{\mathcal{S}}_{s}
and is oriented in the direction of
{\mathcal{F}}^{-s}({\mathbb{H}}_{p})
with
p\geqslant k
. In particular, we construct
\gamma
so that
{\mathcal{F}}^{s}(\gamma)\subseteq{\mathcal{F}}^{s}(W_{e})
is a curve starting at
{\mathcal{F}}^{s}(x)\in\mathbb{S}_{k}
, oriented in a way that
{\mathcal{F}}^{s}(\gamma)\subseteq\cup_{p\geqslant k}{\mathbb{H}}_{p}
and such that it terminates on the first intersection with
{\mathcal{F}}^{s}({\mathcal{S}}_{s})={\mathcal{S}}_{-s}
. The inclusion
{\mathcal{F}}^{s}(\gamma)\subseteq\cup_{p\geqslant k}{\mathbb{H}}_{p}
, together with the fact that
{\mathcal{F}}^{s}(\gamma)
is an unstable curve, implies that
|{\mathcal{F}}^{s}(\gamma)|\leqslant C_{\mathrm{cone}}k^{-2}
. Therefore, by (
3.8
), for some
C>0
and
\Lambda_{0}>1
,

|x-z|\leqslant|\gamma|\leqslant C\Lambda_{0}^{-s}|{\mathcal{F}}^{s}(\gamma)|\leqslant CC_{\mathrm{cone}}\Lambda_{0}^{-s}k^{-2}\leqslant Ck^{-2}.

Since the above estimate holds for any
x\in W\cap{\mathcal{F}}^{-s}(\mathbb{S}_{k})
and for any
s\in\{0,..,n\}
, the equation above concludes the proof.
∎

On a couple of occasions, we will need to compute the effect on the boundary of conditioning on the event of being or not being stacked. Let
{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}=\{(p_{j,\iota},W_{j,\iota},\rho_{j,\iota})\}
be two regular standard families such that
W_{j,1}
and
W_{j,2}
are
\eta
-stacked and let
{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}^{(n)}
the standard family obtained by conditioning on the sets
\widehat{W}_{j,\iota}^{(n)}
and
W_{j,\iota}\setminus\widehat{W}_{j,\iota}^{(n)}
introduced in (
6.33
). More precisely, denote by
\widehat{W}_{j,p,\iota}^{(n)}
and
\overline{W}_{j,q,\iota}^{(n)}
the maximal connected components of
\widehat{W}_{j,\iota}^{(n)}
and
W_{j,\iota}\setminus\widehat{W}_{j,\iota}^{(n)}
(possibly further refined if their
n^{\mathrm{th}}
images are longer than
\delta_{*}
) and set

(6.35)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:conditioning-def}\begin{split}&{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}^{(n)}=\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{p_{j,\iota}\int_{I_{\widehat{W}_{j,p,\iota}^{n}}}\rho_{j,\iota}}{\sum_{j}p_{j,\iota}\int_{I_{\widehat{W}_{j,p,\iota}^{(n)}}}\rho_{j,\iota}},\widehat{W}_{j,p,\iota}^{(n)},\frac{\rho_{j,\iota}{\mathbbm{1}}_{I_{\widehat{W}_{j,p,\iota}^{n}}}}{\int_{\widehat{W}_{j,p,\iota}^{(n)}}\rho_{j,\iota}}\biggr)\biggr\}\quad\text{and}\\
&\overline{{\mathcal{G}}}_{\iota}^{(n)}=\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{p_{j,\iota}\int_{I_{\overline{W}_{j,q,\iota}^{n}}}\rho_{j,\iota}}{\sum_{j}p_{j,\iota}\int_{I_{\overline{W}_{j,q,\iota}^{(n)}}}\rho_{j,\iota}},\overline{W}_{j,q,\iota}^{(n)},\frac{\rho_{j,\iota}{\mathbbm{1}}_{I_{\overline{W}_{j,q,\iota}^{n}}}}{\int_{\overline{W}_{j,q,\iota}^{(n)}}\rho_{j}}\biggr)\biggr\}.\end{split}

Lemma 6.29
.

For any
n,\eta>0
and any two regular standard family
{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}
as above,

\mathcal{Z}(\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{n}{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}^{(n)})\leqslant\frac{C_{\mathrm{cone}}e^{\Omega\varpi_{2}}\mathcal{Z}(\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{n}{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota})}{\sum_{j}p_{j,\iota}\int_{I_{\widehat{W}_{j,p,\iota}^{(n)}}}\rho_{j,\iota}}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{Z}(\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{n}\overline{{\mathcal{G}}}_{\iota}^{(n)})\leqslant\frac{C_{\mathrm{cone}}e^{\Omega\varpi_{2}}\mathcal{Z}(\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{n}{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota})}{\sum_{j}p_{j,\iota}\int_{I_{\overline{W}_{j,q,\iota}^{(n)}}}\rho_{j,\iota}}.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.3
on the continuation of singularity lines, if two points lie in different connected components of
\widehat{W}_{j,\iota}^{(n)}
then they must be separated by a a discontinuity, and hence lie in different components of
W_{j,\iota}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{n,t}^{{\mathbb{H}}}
. Consequentely, each element
\widehat{W}_{j,p,\iota}^{(n)}
corresponds to a unique unstable curve
W_{j,p,\iota}^{n}
in the standard family
\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{n}{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}
such that
{\mathcal{F}}^{n}(\widehat{W}_{j,p,\iota}^{(n)})\subseteq W_{j,p,\iota}^{n}
. Using part c) of Corollary
6.8
,

\int_{I_{\widehat{W}_{j,p,\iota}^{n}}}\rho_{j,\iota}\frac{1}{|\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{n}(\widehat{W}_{j,p,\iota}^{(n)})|}=\int_{I_{\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{n}_{t}(\widehat{W}_{j,p,\iota}^{n})}}{\mathcal{L}}^{n}\rho_{j,\iota}\frac{1}{|\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{n}(\widehat{W}_{j,p,\iota}^{(n)})|}\leqslant C_{\mathrm{cone}}e^{\Omega\varpi_{2}}\int_{I_{W_{j,p,\iota}^{n}}}{\mathcal{L}}^{n}\rho_{j,\iota}\frac{1}{|W_{j,p,\iota}^{n}|}.

Therefore, denoting by
R=\sum_{j}p_{j}\int_{I_{\widehat{W}_{j,p,\iota}^{(n)}}}\rho_{j,\iota}
the total mass of the stacked family, using the definition (
6.35
),

\begin{split}\mathcal{Z}(\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{n}{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}^{(n)})&=R^{-1}\sum_{j}p_{j,\iota}\sum_{p}\int_{I_{\widehat{W}_{j,p,\iota}^{n}}}\rho_{j,\iota}\frac{1}{|\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{n}(\widehat{W}_{j,p,\iota}^{(n)})|}\\
&\leqslant R^{-1}C_{\mathrm{cone}}e^{\Omega\varpi_{2}}\sum_{j}p_{j,\iota}\sum_{p}\int_{I_{W_{j,p,\iota}^{n}}}{\mathcal{L}}^{n}\rho_{j,\iota}\frac{1}{|W_{j,p,\iota}^{n}|}\leqslant R^{-1}C_{\mathrm{cone}}e^{\Omega\varpi_{2}}\mathcal{Z}(\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{n}{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}).\end{split}

The proof of the second inequality is completely analogous with the only difference that each component of
W_{j,\iota}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{n,t}^{{\mathbb{H}}}
corresponds to at most two unstable curves among the family composed by
\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{n}(\overline{W}_{j,p,\iota})
.
∎

We are ready to compute how much mass gets decoupled for two
\eta
-coupled standard families because of discontinuities. We find that if they start sufficiently close, they lose only
{\mathcal{O}}(\eta^{1/2})
mass, independently of
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
. Note however that the decoupled mass has very bad regularity (how bad depends on when it decouples), so that we need to implement some scheme in order to book-keep everything. This is done in Section
6.8
and the net effect is exponential mixing. Recall
\chi\in(0,1/2)
from Lemma
6.24
and (
6.33
) for the notation
\widehat{W}_{\iota}^{(n)}
.

Lemma 6.30
.

There exist
C,\eta_{0}>0
and
k_{0}\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that, for any
\eta<\eta_{0}
,
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, and any two
\eta
-coupled regular standard family
{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}=\{(p_{j},W_{j,\iota},\rho_{j})\}
, for each
\iota\in\{1,2\}
,

\sum_{j}p_{j}\int_{I_{W_{j,\iota}\setminus\widehat{W}^{(n)}_{j,\iota}}}\rho_{j}\leqslant C\eta^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we consider the case
\iota=1
and set
{\mathcal{G}}_{1}=\{(p_{j},W_{j},\rho_{j})\}
. By part c) of Corollary
6.15
, there exists
n_{0}\sim\ln B_{2}
big enough such that
\mathcal{Z}(\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{n_{0}}\widetilde{\mathcal{G}})\leqslant\mathbb{B}_{2}
for any regular standard family
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}
. Let also
n_{0}
be big enough so that Lemma
6.24
is satisfied. We compute the lost mass by iterating
\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{n_{0}}
. According to Lemma
6.26
, we have
W_{j}\setminus\widehat{W}_{j}^{(n_{0})}\subseteq W_{j}^{A}\cup W_{j}^{B}
, where

\begin{split}W_{j}^{A}&=\{x\in W_{j}:\text{ there exists }z\in W_{j}\cap{\mathcal{S}}_{n_{0},t}^{{\mathbb{H}}}\text{ such that }x\in B(z,C_{\mathrm{cone}}\eta)\},\\
W_{j}^{B}&=\{x\in W_{j}:x\in[\partial W_{j}]_{C_{\mathrm{cone}}\eta}\},\end{split}

for some
C_{\mathrm{cone}}>0
big enough. Since
{\mathcal{G}}_{1}
is a regular standard family,
\rho_{j}\in{\mathcal{C}}(\varpi_{2})
and by Lemma
4.1
, we have

(6.36)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:estimate-boundary}\int_{I_{W_{j}^{B}}}\rho_{j}\ \leqslant\frac{C_{\mathrm{cone}}e^{\varpi_{2}}|I_{W_{j}^{B}}|}{|W_{j}|}=\frac{C\eta}{|W_{j}|}.

As for the mass supported on
W_{j}^{A}
, we need to study the intersection of
W_{j}
and
{\mathcal{S}}_{n_{0},t}^{{\mathbb{H}}}
. Recall the enlargements
W_{j,e}
of
W_{j}
defined before Lemma
6.28
and that
\#\{W_{j,e}\cap{\mathcal{S}}_{n_{0},t}\}\leqslant K_{n_{0}}
by Lemma
6.27
. We set

(6.37)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:list-intersection}\begin{split}W_{j,e}\cap{\mathcal{S}}_{n_{0},t}&=\{z_{p}\}_{p\in{\mathcal{I}}_{1}},\quad\#{\mathcal{I}}_{1}\leqslant K_{n_{0}},\\
W_{j}\cap({\mathcal{S}}_{n_{0}}^{{\mathbb{H}}}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{n_{0}})&=W_{j}\cap\bigcup_{k=k_{0}}^{\infty}\bigcup_{s=0}^{n_{0}}{\mathcal{F}}^{-s}(\mathbb{S}_{k})=\{x_{r}\}_{r\in{\mathcal{I}}_{2}},\quad{\mathcal{I}}_{2}\subseteq{\mathbb{N}}.\end{split}

By Lemma
6.28
, there exist
C>0
and
k_{0}>0
such that any
x\in W_{j}\cap\cup_{s=0}^{n_{0}}{\mathcal{F}}^{-s}(\mathbb{S}_{k})
,
k\geqslant k_{0}
, belongs to a
Ck^{-2}
-neighborhood of some
z\in W_{j,e}\cap{\mathcal{S}}_{n_{0},t}
. Therefore for some countable set
\{x_{q_{k,p}}\}_{q_{k,p}}\subseteq W_{j}
, we have

(6.38)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:re-indexing-decoupling}\begin{split}W_{j}\cap({\mathcal{S}}_{n_{0}}^{{\mathbb{H}}}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{n_{0}})\subseteq\bigcup_{p\in{\mathcal{I}}_{1}}&\bigcup_{k=k_{0}}^{\infty}\bigl\{x_{q_{k,p}}\in W_{j}\cap\cup_{s=0}^{n_{0}}{\mathcal{F}}^{-s}(\mathbb{S}_{k}):\text{ }|x_{q_{k,p}}-z_{p}|\leqslant Ck^{-2}\bigr\},\\
q_{k,p}\in{\mathcal{J}}_{k,p},\quad\#{\mathcal{J}}_{k,p}&\leqslant\#\{W_{j}\cap\cup_{s=0}^{n_{0}}{\mathcal{F}}^{-s}(\mathbb{S}_{k})\}.\end{split}

By Lemma
6.27
with
\cup_{s=0}^{n_{0}}{\mathcal{F}}^{-s}(\mathbb{S}_{k})
in place of
{\mathcal{S}}_{n_{0}}\subseteq{\mathcal{S}}_{n_{0},t}
, we have that
\#{\mathcal{J}}_{k,p}\leqslant K_{n_{0}}
for any
k
and
p
. By definition of
W_{j}^{A}
, and equations (
6.37
) and (
6.38
),

W_{j}^{A}\subseteq[W_{j}\cap\cup_{p}B(z_{p},C_{\mathrm{cone}}\eta)]\cup[W_{j}\cap\cup_{p}\cup_{k}\cup_{q_{k,p}}B(x_{q_{k,p}},C_{\mathrm{cone}}\eta)]

and

(6.39)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:bound-density-12}\int_{I_{W_{j}^{A}}}\rho_{j}\leqslant\int_{I_{W_{j}\cap\cup_{p}B(z_{p},C_{\mathrm{cone}}\eta)}}\rho_{j}+\int_{I_{W_{j}\cap\cup_{p}\cup_{k}\cup_{q_{k,p}}B(x_{q_{k,p}},C_{\mathrm{cone}}\eta)}}\rho_{j}.

For the first term, using that
\#{\mathcal{I}}_{1}\leqslant K_{n_{0}}
,

\int_{I_{W_{j}\cap\cup_{p}B(z_{p},C_{cone}\eta)}}\rho_{j}\leqslant\sum_{p\in{\mathcal{I}}_{1}}\int_{I_{W_{j}\cap B(z_{p},C_{cone}\eta)}}\rho_{j}\leqslant\frac{C_{\mathrm{cone}}e^{\varpi_{2}}K_{n_{0}}\eta}{|W_{j}|}=\frac{C_{n_{0}}\eta}{|W_{j}|}.

The second contribution has to do with the mass lost near grazing collisions. On the one hand there are infinite singularities accumulating near a tangent collision, but on the other hand those singularities are closer and closer to each other, so that their contributions to decoupling do not pile up. Setting
k_{*}=\lfloor\eta^{-1/2}\rfloor
, for
k\geqslant k_{*}
and
x\in B(x_{q_{k,p}},C_{\mathrm{cone}}\eta)
, we have

|x-z_{p}|\leqslant|x-x_{q_{k,p}}|+|x_{q_{k,p}}-z_{p}|\leqslant C_{\mathrm{cone}}\eta+Ck_{*}^{-2}\leqslant(C_{\mathrm{cone}}+C)\eta=C\eta,

where in the second inequality we used the defining property (
6.38
) of
x_{q_{k,p}}
. Therefore,

\begin{split}&\cup_{p\in{\mathcal{I}}_{1}}\cup_{k\geqslant k_{0}}\cup_{q_{k,p}\in{\mathcal{J}}_{k,p}}B(x_{q_{k,p}},C_{\mathrm{cone}}\eta)\\
&=\cup_{p\in{\mathcal{I}}_{1}}\bigl(\cup_{k=k_{0}}^{k_{*}}\cup_{q_{k,p}\in{\mathcal{J}}_{k,p}}B(x_{q_{k,p}},C_{\mathrm{cone}}\eta)\cup\cup_{k=k_{*}+1}^{\infty}\cup_{q_{k,p}\in{\mathcal{J}}_{k,p}}B(x_{q_{k,p}},C_{\mathrm{cone}}\eta)\bigr)\\
&\subseteq\cup_{p\in{\mathcal{I}}_{1}}\bigl(\cup_{k=k_{0}}^{\lfloor\eta^{-1/2}\rfloor}\cup_{q_{k,p}\in{\mathcal{J}}_{k,p}}B(x_{q_{k,p}},C_{\mathrm{cone}}\eta)\cup B(z_{p},C\eta)\bigr).\end{split}

Hence, we have the bound,

(6.40)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:second-term-decouples}\begin{split}\int_{I_{W_{j}\cap\cup_{p}\cup_{k}\cup_{q_{k}}B(x_{q_{k,p}},C_{\mathrm{cone}}\eta)}}\rho_{j}&\leqslant\sum_{p\in{\mathcal{I}}_{1}}\sum_{k=k_{0}}^{\lfloor\eta^{-1/2}\rfloor}\sum_{q_{k,p}\in{\mathcal{J}}_{k,p}}\int_{I_{W_{j}\cap B(x_{q_{k,p}},C_{\mathrm{cone}}\eta)}}\rho_{j}\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt+\sum_{p\in{\mathcal{I}}_{1}}\int_{I_{W_{j}\cap B(z_{p},C\eta)}}\rho_{j}.\end{split}

Using that the standard pair is regular and recalling that
\#{\mathcal{I}}_{1}\leqslant K_{n_{0}}
,
\#{\mathcal{J}}_{k,p}\leqslant K_{n_{0}}
, the quantity above is less than, for some
C_{n_{0}}>0
,

\frac{e^{\varpi_{2}}K_{n_{0}}(C_{\mathrm{cone}}K_{n_{0}}\eta^{-\frac{1}{2}}\eta+C\eta)}{|W_{j}|}=\frac{C_{n_{0}}\eta^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|W_{j}|}.

Collecting the previous estimates, we have obtained, for any standard pair in the family,

\int_{I_{W_{j}\setminus\widehat{W}^{(n_{0})}_{j}}}\rho_{j}\leqslant\frac{C_{n_{0}}\eta^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|W_{j}|}.

Summing over
j
and using the fact that
{\mathcal{G}}_{1}
is a regular standard family, we have

(6.41)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:lost-mass-one-step}\sum_{j}p_{j}\int_{I_{W_{j}\setminus\widehat{W}^{(n_{0})}_{j}}}\rho_{j}\leqslant C_{n_{0}}\eta^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{j}p_{j}\frac{1}{|W_{j}|}\leqslant C_{n_{0}}B_{2}\eta^{\frac{1}{2}}.

We now upgrade the result to any
n
. Let
\eta_{0}>0
be so small that
C_{n_{0}}B_{2}\eta_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}<1/2
. Recall (
6.35
) for the definition of conditioning on the stacked part. In particular,
{\mathcal{G}}^{(kn_{0})}_{1}
are the standard families obtained by conditioning
{\mathcal{G}}_{1}
on
\widehat{W}_{j}^{(kn_{0})}
, i.e., on being the part that will be stacked in
\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{kn_{0}}_{t}{\mathcal{G}}_{1}
. By Lemma
6.24
, the curves in
\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{kn_{0}}_{t}{\mathcal{G}}^{(kn_{0})}_{1}
are
\chi^{k}\eta
-stacked with the other standard family
\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{kn_{0}}_{t}{\mathcal{G}}_{2}^{(kn_{0})}
. We first claim that
\mathcal{Z}(\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{kn_{0}}_{t}{\mathcal{G}}^{(kn_{0})}_{1})\leqslant B_{2}
for any
k\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}
. For
k=0
the statement follows from the fact that
\mathcal{Z}({\mathcal{G}}^{(0)}_{1})=\mathcal{Z}({\mathcal{G}}_{1})\leqslant B_{2}
. Assume that the statement holds for some
k
. Then, by Lemma
6.29
and applying (
6.41
) to the regular standard family
\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{kn_{0}}_{t}{\mathcal{G}}_{1}^{(kn_{0})}=\{(\widetilde{p}_{j},\widetilde{W}_{j},\widetilde{\rho}_{j})\}
, we obtain

\begin{split}\mathcal{Z}(\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{(k+1)n_{0}}_{t}{\mathcal{G}}^{((k+1)n_{0})}_{1})&=\mathcal{Z}(\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{n_{0}}(\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{kn_{0}}_{t}{\mathcal{G}}_{1}^{(kn_{0})})^{(n_{0})})\leqslant\frac{C_{\mathrm{cone}}e^{\Omega\varpi_{2}}\mathcal{Z}(\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{n_{0}}(\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{kn_{0}}_{t}{\mathcal{G}}_{1}^{(kn_{0})}))}{\sum_{j}\widetilde{p}_{j}\int_{I_{\widehat{\widetilde{W}}^{(n_{0})}_{j}}}\widetilde{\rho}_{j}}\\[8.0pt]
&\leqslant\frac{C_{\mathrm{cone}}e^{\Omega\varpi_{2}}\mathcal{Z}(\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{n_{0}}(\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{kn_{0}}_{t}{\mathcal{G}}_{1}^{(kn_{0})}))}{1-\sum_{j}\widetilde{p}_{j}\int_{I_{\widetilde{W}_{j}\setminus\widehat{\widetilde{W}}^{(n_{0})}_{j}}}\widetilde{\rho}_{j}}\leqslant\frac{C_{\mathrm{cone}}e^{\Omega\varpi_{2}}\mathbb{B}_{2}}{1-C_{n_{0}}B_{2}\eta^{\frac{1}{2}}}\leqslant B_{2}.\end{split}

In the last inequality we have used (
6.18
) and the fact that
\eta_{0}
is small and in the second to last we used that
n_{0}\sim\ln B_{2}
and the inductive hypothesis. This concludes the proof of the claim. Denote by
m_{k}\in[0,1]
the proportion of mass in
{\mathcal{G}}_{1}
that is supported on
{\mathcal{G}}_{1}^{(kn_{0})}
. Because
\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{kn_{0}}{\mathcal{G}}_{1}^{(kn_{0})}
are regular standard families, we can apply the argument before (
6.41
) to these standard families and, by the mentioned equation, the mass lost from step
k
to step
k+1
is at most
m_{k}C_{n_{0}}B_{2}\eta^{1/2}
. Therefore, for any
q
,

\begin{split}\sum_{j}p_{j}\int_{I_{W_{j}\setminus\widehat{W}^{(qn_{0})}_{j}}}\rho_{j}\leqslant\sum_{k=0}^{q}m_{k}C_{n_{0}}B_{2}(\chi^{k}\eta)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leqslant\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}m_{k}C_{n_{0}}B_{2}(\chi^{k}\eta)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leqslant C_{n_{0}}\eta^{\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

In the second inequality we used that
m_{k}\leqslant 1
. To conclude, recall that
n_{0}\sim\ln B_{2}
and
B_{2}
depends on the table and on the initial conditions
\varpi_{1},B_{1}
only, so that the above quantity is less than
C\eta^{1/2}
. Since for a general
n
the proportion of mass supported on
{\mathcal{G}}_{1}^{(n)}
is more than the proportion of mass supported on
{\mathcal{G}}_{1}^{(qn_{0})}
for
q>n/n_{0}
, the last equation concludes the proof of the Lemma.
∎

6.6.
Decoupling because of the mismatch of the densities

In this section we compute how much mass gets ‘decoupled’ because of the mismatch between the evolved densities of two coupled standard pairs.

Let
\ell_{\iota}=(W_{\iota},\rho)
be any two
\eta
-coupled regular standard pairs and recall that
\widehat{W}^{(n)}_{\iota}\subseteq\widehat{W}_{\iota}
is such that
\mathbb{h}_{n}(\widehat{W}^{(n)}_{\iota})=\widehat{W}^{(n)}_{3-\iota}
. In this section, we let
V_{\iota}
be any connected subsets of
\widehat{W}_{\iota}^{(n)}
and, for
k\in\{0,...,n\}
, set
V_{\iota,k}={\mathcal{F}}^{k}(V_{\iota})
and
I=I_{V_{1,n}}=I_{V_{2,n}}
. The following result is essentially a finite time version of
[
21
, Theorem 5.42]
. Recall the functions
r^{n}_{1,W_{\iota}}
from (
6.2
).

Lemma 6.31
.

There exists
C>0
such that, for any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
\eta>0
, for any
r\in I
,

\biggl|\ln\frac{{r_{1,V_{1,n}}^{-n}}^{\prime}(r)}{{r_{1,V_{2,n}}^{-n}}^{\prime}(r)}\biggr|\leqslant C\eta^{\frac{1}{3}}.

Proof.

To any
r\in I
it corresponds a point
x_{\iota}=(r,\varphi_{V_{\iota,n}}(r))
in
V_{\iota,n}
. For
k\in\{1,...,n\}
, set
x_{k}={\mathcal{F}}^{-k}(r,\varphi_{V_{1,n}}(r))
,
\overline{x}_{k}={\mathcal{F}}^{-k}(r,\varphi_{V_{2,n}}(r))
and call
x_{k}=(r_{k},\varphi_{k})
,
\overline{x}_{k}=(\overline{r}_{k},\overline{\varphi}_{k})
. Then

(6.42)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:log-series-hol}\ln\frac{{r_{1,V_{1,n}}^{-n}}^{\prime}(r)}{{r_{1,V_{2,n}}^{-n}}^{\prime}(r)}=\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\ln r_{1,V_{2,k}}^{\prime}(r_{n-k})-\ln r_{1,V_{1,k}}^{\prime}(\overline{r}_{n-k}).

Using the equation (
6.3
) for derivative of
r_{1,W}
and setting also
\tau_{j}=\tau(x_{j})
,
\kappa_{j}=\kappa(x_{j})
and
\overline{\tau}_{j}=\tau(\overline{x}_{j})
,
\overline{\kappa}_{j}=\kappa(\overline{x}_{j})
, we have

(6.43)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:log-1}\begin{split}\ln r_{1,V_{1,k}}^{\prime}(r_{n-k})&=\ln\cos\varphi_{n-k-1}\\
&-\ln(\tau_{n-k}\kappa_{n-k}+\cos\varphi_{n-k}+\tau_{n-k}\varphi_{V_{1,k}}^{\prime}(r_{n-k}))\end{split}

and similarly

(6.44)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:log-2}\begin{split}\ln r_{1,V_{2,k}}^{\prime}(\overline{r}_{n-k})&=\ln\cos\overline{\varphi}_{n-k-1}\\
&-\ln(\overline{\tau}_{k-n}\overline{\kappa}_{n-k}+\cos\overline{\varphi}_{n-k}+\overline{\tau}_{n-k}\varphi_{V_{2,k}}^{\prime}(\overline{r}_{n-k})).\end{split}

Let
H_{0}\subseteq{\mathcal{H}}_{n}(x_{n})
be the curve connecting
x_{n}
to
\mathbb{h}_{n}(x_{n})
. Set
H_{k}={\mathcal{F}}^{k}(H_{0})
and
\delta_{k}=|H_{k}|
. By the definition (
6.32
) of fake holonomy, we have that
H_{k}
is weakly homogeneous for
k\in\{0,1,...,n\}
. Recalling that
x_{n-k},\overline{x}_{n-k}\in H_{k}
, there exists
C>0
such that

\begin{split}|\ln\cos\varphi_{n-k-1}-\ln\cos\overline{\varphi}_{n-k-1}|&\leqslant C\frac{|\varphi_{n-k-1}-\overline{\varphi}_{n-k-1}|}{\cos\varphi_{n-k-1}}\leqslant C\frac{\delta_{k+1}}{\delta_{k+1}^{\frac{2}{3}}}\leqslant C\delta_{k+1}^{\frac{1}{3}},\end{split}

where in the second inequality we used Lemma
3.2
. Denoting by

\gamma_{k}=|\varphi_{V_{1,k}}^{\prime}(r_{n-k})-\varphi_{V_{2,k}}^{\prime}(\overline{r}_{n-k})|

the angle between the tangent vectors to the unstable curves
V_{\iota,k}
and using that the argument of the logarithms in (
6.43
) and (
6.44
) are bounded from below, we obtain

(6.45)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:exp-decreasing-hol}|\ln r_{1,V_{1,k}}^{\prime}(r_{n-k})-\ln r_{1,V_{2,k}}^{\prime}(\overline{r}_{n-k})|\leqslant C(\delta_{k+1}^{\frac{1}{3}}+\delta_{k}+\gamma_{k}).

Here we also used that
\tau
and
\overline{\tau}
are
{\mathcal{C}}^{1}
as a function of
r
and
r_{1}
on
V_{\iota,k}\subseteq{\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{-1}^{{\mathbb{H}}}
(see (
5.8
)) and so

|\tau_{n-k}-\overline{\tau}_{n-k}|=|\tau(r_{n-k},r_{n-k-1})-\tau(\overline{r}_{n-k},\overline{r}_{n-k-1})|\leqslant C(\delta_{k}+\delta_{k+1}).

By (
6.34
) and Lemma
6.25
there exists
\Lambda>1
,
C>0
such that
\delta_{k}\leqslant C\Lambda^{-k}\delta_{0}
and
\gamma_{k}\leqslant C(\delta_{0}k/\Lambda^{k}+\gamma_{0}/\Lambda^{k})
. Therefore, by (
6.45
) and (
6.42
), there exists
C>0
such that, for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,

\biggl|\ln\frac{{r_{1,V_{1,n}}^{-n}}^{\prime}(r)}{{r_{1,V_{2,n}}^{-n}}^{\prime}(r)}\biggr|\leqslant C(\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{3}}+\gamma_{0}).

Since
W_{\iota}
are
\eta
-stacked and
H_{0}
is a stable curve,
\delta_{0}\leqslant C_{\mathrm{cone}}\eta
. Moreover,

\gamma_{0}=|\varphi_{W_{1}}^{\prime}(r_{n})-\varphi_{W_{2}}^{\prime}(\overline{r}_{n})|\leqslant|\varphi_{W_{1}}^{\prime}(r_{n})-\varphi_{W_{2}}^{\prime}(r_{n})|+|\varphi_{W_{2}}^{\prime}(r_{n})-\varphi_{W_{2}}^{\prime}(\overline{r}_{n})|,

and the first quantity is less than
\eta
because
W_{\iota}
are
\eta
-stacked while the second is less than
C\eta
because
|\varphi_{W_{2}}^{\prime\prime}|
is bounded and

(6.46)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:displacemet-holonomy}|r_{n}-\overline{r}_{n}|\leqslant|H_{0}|\leqslant C_{\mathrm{cone}}\eta.

This concludes the proof of the statement.
∎

If we start with two
\eta
-coupled standard pairs their densities don’t undergo the same evolution under
{\mathcal{L}}
because they are supported on different curves. However, since the curves are close, the evolutions almost coincide.

Lemma 6.32
.

There exists
C>0
such that, for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
\eta>0
, for any
r\in I
,

\biggl|\ln\frac{{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{1,n}}\rho(r)}{{\mathcal{L}}_{V_{2,n}}^{n}\rho(r)}\biggr|\leqslant C\eta^{\frac{1}{3}}.

Proof.

Recalling (
6.6
),

\biggl|\ln\frac{{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{1,n}}\rho(r)}{{\mathcal{L}}_{V_{2,n}}^{n}\rho(r)}\biggr|\leqslant\biggl|\ln\frac{\rho\circ r_{1,V_{1,n}}^{-n}(r)}{\rho\circ r_{1,V_{2,n}}^{-n}(r)}\biggr|+\biggl|\ln\frac{{r_{1,V_{1,n}}^{-n}}^{\prime}(r)}{{r_{1,V_{2,n}}^{-n}}^{\prime}(r)}\biggr|.

Denote as before
(r_{n},\varphi_{n})={\mathcal{F}}^{-n}(r,\varphi_{V_{1,n}}(r))
,
(\overline{r}_{n},\overline{\varphi}_{n})={\mathcal{F}}^{-n}(r,\varphi_{V_{2,n}}(r))
. Using the fact that
\rho\in{\mathcal{C}}(\varpi_{2})
and (
6.46
), there exists
C>0
such that for all
n
and
\eta
,

\biggl|\ln\frac{\rho\circ r_{1,V_{1,n}}^{-n}(r)}{\rho\circ r_{1,V_{2,n}}^{-n}(r)}\biggr|\leqslant\varpi_{2}|r_{n}-\overline{r}_{n}|^{\frac{1}{3}}\leqslant C\eta^{\frac{1}{3}}.

This, together with Lemma
6.31
, concludes the proof of the statement.
∎

We now split the push-forward of the densities supported by the two curves into a coupled part
\rho_{*}
and uncoupled parts
\rho_{1}
and
\rho_{2}
. We show that the mass that we are not able to couple is controlled by some power of the distance
\eta
between the original standard pairs. We find that the coupled density is regular while the other two densities (which we call
leftover densities
) belong to the very bad cone
{\mathcal{C}}(C\eta^{-1})
. This does not compromise our coupling scheme as these densities recover after
\sim\ln\eta^{-1}
iterations. During this transient, before they recover, we find that their oscillations (precisely their
\max/\min
ratio) remain of order one because of Lemma
6.9
. Leveraging on this fact, we can control the evolution of the boundary
\mathcal{Z}
of standard pairs supporting leftover densities.

Lemma 6.33
.

There exists
C>0
such that for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
big enough and
\eta>0
small enough, there exist
\rho_{*},\rho_{1},\rho_{2}:I\to{\mathbb{R}}^{+}
such that

\begin{split}&\frac{{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{1,n}}\rho}{\int_{I}{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{1,n}}\rho}=\rho_{*}+\rho_{1},\quad\frac{{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{2,n}}\rho}{\int_{I}{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{2,n}}\rho}=\rho_{*}+\rho_{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\int_{I}\rho_{*}\geqslant 1-C\eta^{\frac{1}{3}}.\end{split}

Moreover,
\rho_{*}\in{\mathcal{C}}(\varpi_{2})
and, for
\iota\in\{1,2\}
,
\rho_{\iota}\in{\mathcal{C}}(C\eta^{-1})
and
\max\rho_{\iota}/\min\rho_{\iota}\leqslant 2e^{\varpi_{2}}
.

Proof.

Let
\overline{n}\in{\mathbb{N}}
be given by part b) of Corollary
6.8
and consider
n\geqslant\overline{n}
. For some
c_{0}>0
to be fixed shortly, we define

\begin{split}\rho_{*}&=\min\biggl\{\frac{{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{1,n}}\rho}{\int_{I}{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{1,n}}\rho},\frac{{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{2,n}}\rho}{\int_{I}{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{2,n}}\rho}\biggr\}(1-c_{0}\eta^{\frac{1}{3}}),\\
\rho_{1}&=\frac{{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{1,n}}\rho}{\int_{I}{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{1,n}}\rho}-\rho_{*}\quad\text{and}\quad\rho_{2}=\frac{{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{2,n}}\rho}{\int_{I}{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{2,n}}\rho}-\rho_{*}.\end{split}

By Lemma
6.32
, there exists
C>0
such that, for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
\eta>0
small enough,

\ \frac{\int_{I}{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{1,n}}\rho}{\int_{I}{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{2,n}}\rho}=\int_{I}({\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{1,n}}\rho/{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{2,n}}\rho)\frac{{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{2,n}}\rho}{\int_{I}{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{2,n}}\rho}\geqslant 1-C\eta^{\frac{1}{3}}.

Hence, using Lemma
6.32
again, for any
r\in I
and
\eta
small enough,

\frac{{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{2,n}}\rho\int_{I}{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{1,n}}\rho}{{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{1,n}}\rho\int_{I}{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{2,n}}\rho}\geqslant 1-2C\eta^{\frac{1}{3}}.

Renaming the constant, we have obtained that, for some
C>0
,

(6.47)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:estimate-min-density-fraction}\min\biggl\{1,\frac{{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{2,n}}\rho\int_{I}{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{1,n}}\rho}{{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{1,n}}\rho\int_{I}{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{2,n}}\rho}\biggr\}\geqslant 1-C\eta^{\frac{1}{3}}.

Set
c_{0}
to be equal to the constant
C
in (
6.47
). Then,

\begin{split}\int_{I}\rho_{*}&=(1-c_{0}\eta^{\frac{1}{3}})\int_{I}\frac{{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{1,n}}\rho}{\int_{I}{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{1,n}}\rho}\min\biggl\{1,\frac{{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{2,n}}\rho\int_{I}{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{1,n}}\rho}{{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{1,n}}\rho\int_{I}{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{2,n}}\rho}\biggr\}\\
&\geqslant(1-c_{0}\eta^{\frac{1}{3}})(1-c_{0}\eta^{\frac{1}{3}})\geqslant 1-2c_{0}\eta^{\frac{1}{3}}.\end{split}

This proves the lower bound on the amount of coupled mass. The fact that
\rho_{*}\in{\mathcal{C}}(\varpi_{2})
is a consequence of the definition of
\rho_{*}
and Lemma
6.10
. As for the leftover densities, we have

\begin{split}\min\rho_{1}&\geqslant\min_{I}\frac{{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{1,n}}\rho}{\int_{I}{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{1,n}}\rho}\biggl(1-\max_{I}\min\biggl\{1,\frac{{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{2,n}}\rho\int_{I}{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{1,n}}\rho}{{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{1,n}}\rho\int_{I}{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{2,n}}\rho}\biggr\}(1-c_{0}\eta^{\frac{1}{3}})\biggr)\\
&\geqslant\frac{e^{-\delta_{*}^{1/3}\omega_{2}/2}}{|I|}[1-(1-c_{0}\eta^{\frac{1}{3}})]\geqslant\frac{c_{0}e^{-\omega_{2}/2}\eta^{\frac{1}{3}}}{|I|}.\end{split}

In the second inequality we have used that
{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{1,n}}\rho\in{\mathcal{C}}(\varpi_{2}/2)
, which is true because of the choice of
n
specified at the beginning of the proof. Using (
6.47
) again and the choice of
c_{0}
,

\begin{split}\max\rho_{1}&\leqslant\max_{I}\frac{{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{1,n}}\rho}{\int_{I}{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{1,n}}\rho}\biggl(1-\min_{I}\min\biggl\{1,\frac{{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{2,n}}\rho\int_{I}{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{1,n}}\rho}{{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{1,n}}\rho\int_{I}{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{2,n}}\rho}\biggr\}(1-c_{0}\eta^{\frac{1}{3}})\biggr)\\
&\leqslant\frac{e^{\delta_{*}^{1/3}\omega_{2}/2}}{|I|}\biggl(1-(1-c_{0}\eta^{\frac{1}{3}})(1-c_{0}\eta^{\frac{1}{3}})\biggr)\leqslant\frac{2e^{\omega_{2}/2}c_{0}\eta^{\frac{1}{3}}}{|I|}.\end{split}

Thus,
\max\rho_{1}/\min\rho_{1}\leqslant 2e^{\omega_{2}}
. The estimate for
\rho_{2}
is analogous. Finally, for
\eta
small enough,

\frac{{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{1,n}}\rho}{\rho_{*}\int_{I}{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{1,n}}\rho}=\frac{1}{(1-c_{0}\eta^{\frac{1}{3}})\min\biggl\{1,\frac{{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{2,n}}\rho\int_{I}{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{1,n}}\rho}{{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{1,n}}\rho\int_{I}{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{2,n}}\rho}\biggr\}}\geqslant 1+\frac{c_{0}}{2}\eta^{\frac{1}{3}}.

Therefore, by Lemma
6.10
,
\rho_{1}\in{\mathcal{C}}(\varpi_{*})
with

\varpi_{*}=\varpi_{2}\biggl(1+2\sup\frac{1}{\frac{{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{1,n}}\rho}{\rho_{*}\int_{I}{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{V_{1,n}}\rho}-1}\biggr)=\varpi_{2}\biggl(1+4\frac{1}{c_{0}\eta^{\frac{1}{3}}}\biggr)\leqslant C\eta^{-\frac{1}{3}}.

This concludes the proof.
∎

6.7.
Evolution of two coupled standard families

In this section we collect the results of Sections
6.5
and
6.6
in a single statement which records how much mass gets decoupled and the time needed to recover. The main result of this section is Lemma
6.39
.

We consider a longer time step for the dynamics. This is a big enough iterate
N_{c}
of the leaky dynamics such that a list of good properties is met. In particular, we require that

(6.48)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:conditionN_{c}}N_{c}\geqslant\alpha_{1}\ln B_{2}+\alpha_{2},

where
\alpha_{1}
and
\alpha_{2}
are given by Corollary
6.15
. We also assume that
N_{c}
is big enough so that Lemmata
6.7
,
6.24
and
6.33
apply. Informally, at the time scale
N_{c}
stacked curves become even more stacked and the regularity data
(\varpi,B)
have enough time to see contraction. We fix the precise value of
N_{c}
in the next section, only after fixing the length scale
\eta_{0}
for coupling, in a way that some mass becomes coupled each time step. By Lemma
6.16
, we can set
t_{0}>0
small enough (depending on
N_{c}
) such that,

(6.49)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:condition-on-t_{0}}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}}({\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}})\geqslant\frac{3}{4},

for all
t\in[0,t_{0}]
and
\iota\in\{1,2\}
. To sum up, in the next section, we first fix
\eta_{0}>0
small enough and consider
\eta\leqslant\eta_{0}
, then we fix
N_{c}>0
sufficiently big, depending on
\eta_{0}
, and finally we set
t_{0}
small enough depending on
N_{c}
. To keep the presentation readable, the explicit order of all the quantifiers is specified only in Lemma
6.39
, which summarizes all the results of this section. We now introduce
regularity classes

\mathfrak{R}_{k}
. These are a tool to book-keep very irregular mass and the time needed to become regular again.

Definition 6.34
.

We say that a standard family
{\mathcal{G}}
belongs to
\mathfrak{R}_{k}
,
k\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}
, if
\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{kN_{c}}{\mathcal{G}}
is a regular standard family.

Note that Definition
6.34
does not include the hole but only its derived discontinuities, even if ultimately we are interested in exponential mixing for the leaky evolution. Because of the Growth Lemma, mass with vary bad regularity takes only moderate time to recover.

Lemma 6.35
.

There exists
C>0
such that the following is true. Let
{\mathcal{G}}=\{(p_{k},W_{k},\rho_{k})\}
be a standard family such that
\max\rho_{k}/\min\rho_{k}\leqslant 2e^{\varpi_{2}}
for each
k
. For all
0<\eta<1/2
, if either
{\mathcal{G}}
is a
(\omega_{2},\eta^{-2})
-standard family or a
(\eta^{-2},B_{2})
-standard family, then
{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathfrak{R}_{\lfloor C\ln\eta^{-1}\rfloor}
.

Proof.

Take a
(\omega_{2},\eta^{-2})
-standard family
{\mathcal{G}}
. Since
N_{c}\geqslant\overline{n}
, by Lemma
6.7
the standard families
\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{nN_{c}}{\mathcal{G}}
support densities in
{\mathcal{C}}(\varpi_{2})
for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
. Moreover, by part c) of Corollary
6.15
, for any
k\geqslant\alpha_{1}\ln\eta^{-2}+\alpha_{2}
, we have that
\mathcal{Z}(\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{kN_{c}}{\mathcal{G}})\leqslant\mathbb{B}_{2}\ll B_{2}
. Since
\alpha_{1}\ln\eta^{-2}+\alpha_{2}\leqslant\lfloor C\ln\eta^{-1}\rfloor
for
C>0
big enough, this proves the first part of the statement. The second part follows in a similar way using part b) of Corollary
6.8
and the invariance of
\mathcal{Z}({\mathcal{G}})
guaranteed by Lemma
6.14
and the assumption on the initial densities.
∎

Let
{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}=\{(p_{j},W_{j,\iota},\rho_{j})\}
be two
\eta
-coupled regular standard families and denote by
W_{j,p,\iota}
the connected component of
W_{j,\iota}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathbb{H}}}_{N_{c},t}
such that
W_{j,p,\iota}\subset{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}}
. By further subdividing them, we require as usual that
|{\mathcal{F}}^{N_{c}}(W_{j,p,\iota})|\leqslant\delta_{*}
. Denote by
\widehat{W}_{j,p,\iota}=\widehat{W}_{j,p,\iota}^{(N_{c})}\subseteq W_{j,p,\iota}
the maximal connected subsets of
W_{j,\iota}
where
\mathbb{h}_{N_{c}}
is well defined and such that
\mathbb{h}_{N_{c}}(\widehat{W}_{j,p,1})=\widehat{W}_{j,p,2}
. Finally, call
\overline{W}_{j,q,\iota}
the connected components of
W_{j,p,\iota}\setminus\widehat{W}_{j,p,\iota}
such that
\overline{W}_{j,q,\iota}\subseteq{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}}
. We are considering all the mass in
\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{N_{c}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}
that will not end up in the hole in the next
N_{c}
iterations and we are further subdividing it according to whether it is supported on curves that are getting stacked or not. We now introduce two standard families
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}
and
\overline{{\mathcal{G}}}_{\iota}
. These are
essentially
obtained by conditioning
{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}
on the events
\widehat{W}_{j,p,\iota}
and
\overline{W}_{j,p,\iota}
. However, in order to have coupled standard families, we need to make sure that
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{1}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{2}
have the same weights, and so we need to declare uncoupled some mass supported on
\widehat{W}_{j,p,\iota}
.
More precisely, call

\begin{split}&\Omega_{c}=\sum_{j}p_{j}\sum_{p}\min_{\iota\in\{1,2\}}\int_{I_{\widehat{W}_{j,p,\iota}}}\rho_{j}\quad\text{and}\\
&\Omega_{u,\iota}=\sum_{j}p_{j}\sum_{q}\int_{I_{\overline{W}_{j,q,\iota}}}\rho_{j}+\sum_{j}p_{j}\sum_{p}\biggl(\int_{\widehat{W}_{j,p,\iota}}\rho_{j}-\min_{\iota\in\{1,2\}}\int_{I_{\widehat{W}_{j,p,\iota}}}\rho_{j}\biggr),\end{split}

the coupled and non-coupled mass respectively. Since some mass might end up in the hole,
\Omega_{c}
and
\Omega_{u,\iota}
don’t necessarily sum up to one. We set

(6.50)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:almost-coupled-def}\begin{split}&\widehat{\rho}_{j,p,\iota}=\frac{\rho_{j}{\mathbbm{1}}_{I_{\widehat{W}_{j,p,\iota}}}}{\int_{I_{\widehat{W}_{j,p,\iota}}}\rho_{j}},\quad\widehat{s}_{j,p}=\frac{p_{j}\min_{\iota\in\{1,2\}}\int_{I_{\widehat{W}_{j,p,\iota}}}\rho_{j}}{\Omega_{c}},\\
&\overline{\rho}_{j,q,\iota}=\frac{\rho_{j}{\mathbbm{1}}_{I_{\overline{W}_{j,q,\iota}}}}{\int_{I_{\overline{W}_{j,q,\iota}}}\rho_{j}},\quad\overline{s}_{j,q,\iota}=\frac{p_{j}\int_{I_{\overline{W}_{j,q,\iota}}}\rho_{j}}{\Omega_{u,\iota}},\end{split}

and we consider the standard families

\begin{split}&\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}=\{(\widehat{s}_{j,p},\widehat{W}_{j,p,\iota},\widehat{\rho}_{j,p,\iota})\}\quad\text{and}\\[10.0pt]
&\overline{{\mathcal{G}}}_{\iota}=\{(\overline{s}_{j,q,\iota},\overline{W}_{j,q,\iota},\overline{\rho}_{j,q,\iota})\}\cup\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{p_{j}\bigl(\int_{I_{\widehat{W}_{j,p,\iota}}}\rho_{j}-\min_{\iota\in\{1,2\}}\int_{I_{\widehat{W}_{j,p,\iota}}}\rho_{j}\bigr)}{\Omega_{u,\iota}},\widehat{W}_{j,p,\iota},\widehat{\rho}_{j,p,\iota}\biggr)\biggr\}.\end{split}

Since
{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}
are regular standard families, by Lemma
6.30
,

\begin{split}\sum_{j}p_{j}\sum_{p}\int_{I_{\overline{W}_{j,p,\iota}}}\rho_{j}\leqslant\sum_{j}p_{j}\int_{I_{W_{j,\iota}\setminus\widehat{W}_{j,\iota}}}\rho_{j}&\leqslant C\eta^{\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

and, using Lemma
6.32
and denoting by
I_{j,p}=I_{{\mathcal{F}}^{n}(\widehat{W}_{j,p,1})}=I_{{\mathcal{F}}^{n}(\widehat{W}_{j,p,2})}
, for any
\eta>0
small enough, we have that

(6.51)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:difference-stacked-mass}\begin{split}\sum_{j}p_{j}\sum_{p}&\biggl|\int_{I_{\widehat{W}_{j,p,1}}}\rho_{j}-\int_{I_{\widehat{W}_{j,p,2}}}\rho_{j}\biggr|=\sum_{j}p_{j}\sum_{p}\int_{I_{\widehat{W}_{j,p,2}}}\rho_{j}\biggl|\frac{\int_{I_{j,p}}{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{{\mathcal{F}}^{n}(\widehat{W}_{j,p,1})}\rho_{j}}{\int_{I_{j,p}}{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{{\mathcal{F}}^{n}(\widehat{W}_{j,p,2})}\rho_{j}}-1\biggr|\\[8.0pt]
&\leqslant\sum_{j}p_{j}\sum_{p}\int_{I_{\widehat{W}_{j,p,2}}}\rho_{j}\int_{I_{j,p}}\biggl|\frac{{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{{\mathcal{F}}^{n}(\widehat{W}_{j,p,1})}\rho_{j}}{{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{{\mathcal{F}}^{n}(\widehat{W}_{j,p,2})}\rho_{j}}-1\biggr|\frac{{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{{\mathcal{F}}^{n}(\widehat{W}_{j,p,2})}\rho_{j}}{\int_{I_{j,p}}{\mathcal{L}}^{n}_{{\mathcal{F}}^{n}(\widehat{W}_{j,p,2})}\rho_{j}}\leqslant C\eta^{\frac{1}{3}}.\end{split}

Therefore, the non-stacked mass
\Omega_{u,\iota}
, which is bounded by the contributions from the last two equations, is small. In particular, by possibly coupling less mass, we can ensure that, for
\eta
sufficiently small,

(6.52)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:estimate-a}\Omega_{c}=1-\Omega_{u,\iota}-\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}}({\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}})\geqslant\frac{1}{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{C\eta^{\frac{1}{3}}}{2}\leqslant\Omega_{u,\iota}\leqslant C\eta^{\frac{1}{3}}.

For the lower bound for
\Omega_{c}
, we use the upper bound on
\Omega_{u,\iota}
and the fact that the mass that enters the hole is less than
1/4
, according to (
6.49
). Let also
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,N_{c}}=\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{N_{c}}\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}
and
\overline{{\mathcal{G}}}_{\iota,N_{c}}=\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{N_{c}}\overline{{\mathcal{G}}}_{\iota}
. By construction, for any
\phi\in{\mathcal{C}}^{0}({\mathcal{M}})
,

(6.53)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:first-dec}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}}(\phi\circ\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{N_{c}}{\mathbbm{1}}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}}})=\Omega_{c}\mu_{\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,N_{c}}}(\phi)+\Omega_{u,\iota}\mu_{\overline{{\mathcal{G}}}_{\iota,N_{c}}}(\phi).

Lemma 6.36
.

For all
\eta>0
small enough,
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,N_{c}}
are regular standard families. Moreover, there exists
C>0
such that, for all
\eta>0
small enough,
\overline{{\mathcal{G}}}_{\iota,N_{c}}\in\mathfrak{R}_{\lfloor C\ln\eta^{-1}\rfloor}
.

Proof.

By (
6.50
) and the fact that
{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}
are regular standard families, we have that
\widehat{\rho}_{j,p,\iota}
and
\overline{\rho}_{j,q,\iota}
belong to
{\mathcal{C}}(\varpi_{2})
. Consequently, by Lemma
6.7
and the fact that
N_{c}\geqslant\overline{n}
, we have that both
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,N_{c}}
and
\overline{{\mathcal{G}}}_{\iota,N_{c}}
support regular densities. Recalling (
6.35
), denote by
{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}^{(N_{c})}
the standard families obtained by conditioning on being supported on the ‘matched part’ of the holonomy and
\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{N_{c}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}^{(N_{c})}
their images, which coincide with conditioning
\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{N_{c}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}
on the event of being stacked (here we are considering also the mass that ends up in the hole). Then, by Lemma
6.29
and the fact that the decoupled mass is less than
1/2
for
\eta
small enough, we have that
\mathcal{Z}(\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{N_{c}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}^{(N_{c})})\leqslant 2e^{\Omega\varpi_{2}}C_{\mathrm{cone}}\mathcal{Z}(\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{N_{c}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota})
. Therefore, by the choice (
6.48
) of
N_{c}
and part c) of Corollary
6.15
,

\mathcal{Z}(\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{N_{c}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}^{(N_{c})})\leqslant 2e^{\Omega\varpi_{2}}C_{\mathrm{cone}}\mathbb{B}_{2}.

Consequently, using also the lower bound for
\Omega_{c}
in Equation (
6.52
), we obtain that

\begin{split}\mathcal{Z}(\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,N_{c}})&=\mathcal{Z}(\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{N_{c}}\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota})=\sum_{j}\sum_{p}\widehat{s}_{j,p}\frac{1}{|\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{N_{c}}(\widehat{W}_{j,p,\iota})|}=\frac{\sum_{j}p_{j}\sum_{p}\min_{\iota}\int_{I_{\widehat{W}_{j,p,\iota}}}\rho_{j}\frac{1}{|\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{N_{c}}(\widehat{W}_{j,p,\iota})|}}{\Omega_{c}}\\
&\leqslant\frac{\sum_{j}p_{j}\sum_{p}\int_{I_{\widehat{W}_{j,p,\iota}}}\rho_{j}\frac{1}{|\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{N_{c}}(\widehat{W}_{j,p,\iota})|}}{\Omega_{c}}\leqslant\frac{\mathcal{Z}(\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{N_{c}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}^{(N_{c})})}{\Omega_{c}}\leqslant 4e^{\Omega\varpi_{2}}C_{\mathrm{cone}}\mathbb{B}_{2}\leqslant B_{2}.\end{split}

In the second inequality, we used that the sums go over the stacked curves that do not end up in the hole, whereas
\mathcal{Z}(\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{N_{c}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}^{(N_{c})})
contains the contributions from all the stacked curves. This proves that
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,N_{c}}
are regular standard families. As for the second part of the statement, a similar computation yields, for
\eta>0
small enough,
\mathcal{Z}(\overline{{\mathcal{G}}}_{\iota,N_{c}})\lesssim 1/\Omega_{u,\iota}\leqslant C\eta^{-1/3}\leqslant\eta^{-2}
. Therefore,
\overline{{\mathcal{G}}}_{\iota,N_{c}}
is a
(\varpi_{2},\eta^{-2})
-standard family and Lemma
6.35
concludes the proof.
∎

By Lemma
6.24
and because
N_{c}
is big enough, the two standard families
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,N_{c}}
are supported on
\chi\eta
-stacked unstable curves. However their densities
\widehat{\rho}_{j,p,\iota}
may slightly differ. Recall that, according to Definition
6.18
, we need the densities to be equal for declaring two standard pairs coupled. In the next Lemma, we take care of this ‘mismatch’ between the densities and we estimate the regularity class of standard families supporting leftover densities.

Lemma 6.37
.

There exists
C>0
such that, for all
\eta>0
small enough, there exists
b>0
,
b\leqslant C\eta^{1/3}
, such that, for all
t\in[0,t_{0}]
,

\mu_{\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,N_{c}}}=(1-b)\mu_{\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,N_{c},c}}+b\mu_{\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,N_{c},u}},

where
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,N_{c},c}
are
\chi\eta
-coupled regular standard families and
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,N_{c},u}\in\mathfrak{R}_{\lfloor C\ln\eta^{-1}\rfloor}
.

Proof.

Recall that the standard families
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,N_{c}}=\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{N_{c}}\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}
support densities which are push-forwards of the densities
\rho_{j}
in
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}
. By Lemma
6.33
, there exist
\rho^{(c)}_{j,p}\in{\mathcal{C}}(\varpi_{2})
,
\rho^{(u)}_{j,p,\iota}\in{\mathcal{C}}(C\eta^{-1})
with
\max\rho^{(u)}_{j,p,\iota}/\min\rho^{(u)}_{j,p,\iota}\leqslant 2e^{\varpi_{2}}
such that

\begin{split}\frac{{\mathcal{L}}^{N_{c}}\rho_{j}}{\int_{I_{{\mathcal{F}}^{N_{c}}(\widehat{W}_{j,p,\iota})}}{\mathcal{L}}^{N_{c}}\rho_{j}}=\rho^{(c)}_{j,p}+\rho^{(u)}_{j,p,\iota}=\biggl(\int\rho^{(c)}_{j,p}\biggr)\frac{\rho_{j,p}^{(c)}}{\int\rho^{(c)}_{j,p}}+\biggl(\int\rho^{(u)}_{j,p,\iota}\biggr)\frac{\rho^{(u)}_{j,p,\iota}}{\int\rho^{(u)}_{j,p,\iota}}.\end{split}

Introducing
b=\sup_{j}\int\rho^{(u)}_{j,p,\iota}
and
\widetilde{\rho}^{(u)}_{j,p,\iota}
, which is some weighted average of
\rho^{(u)}_{j,p,\iota}/(\int\rho^{(u)}_{j,p,\iota})
and
\rho^{(c)}_{j,p,\iota}/(\int\rho^{(c)}_{j,p,\iota})
, we have, for all
j,p
,

(6.54)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:dec-densities}\frac{{\mathcal{L}}^{N_{c}}\rho_{j}}{\int_{I_{{\mathcal{F}}^{N_{c}}(\widehat{W}_{j,p,\iota})}}{\mathcal{L}}^{N_{c}}\rho_{j}}=(1-b)\frac{\rho_{j,p}^{(c)}}{\int\rho^{(c)}_{j,p}}+b\widetilde{\rho}^{(u)}_{j,p,\iota}.

The fact that
b=\sup_{j}\int\rho^{(u)}_{j,p,\iota}=1-\inf_{j}\int\rho^{(c)}_{j,p}\leqslant C\eta^{1/3}
is also part of Lemma
6.33
. Moreover,
\widetilde{\rho}^{(u)}_{j,p,\iota}
belongs to
{\mathcal{C}}(C\eta^{-1})
as well, because
\rho_{j,p,\iota}^{(u)}
does and
\rho_{j,p}^{(c)}
belongs to the much better cone
{\mathcal{C}}(\varpi_{2})
. Equation (
6.54
) induces a decomposition for the standard families
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,N_{c}}
, which we denote with the same notation as in the statement of the Lemma. By Lemma
6.24
and the choice of
N_{c}
, the curves
{\mathcal{F}}^{N_{c}}(\widehat{W}_{j,p,\iota})
are
\chi\eta
-stacked and the two standard families
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,N_{c},c}
support the same probability density
\rho^{(c)}_{j,p}/\int\rho^{(c)}_{j,p}
. This proves that
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,N_{c},c}
are
\chi\eta
-coupled. It remains to study the regularity of the standard families. As we already mentioned,
\rho^{(c)}_{j,p}\in{\mathcal{C}}(\varpi_{2})
. Since the decomposition (
6.54
) does not change the unstable curves in the family nor the weights, we have
\mathcal{Z}(\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,N_{c},c})=\mathcal{Z}(\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,N_{c}})
. Moreover, by Lemma
6.36
, we have that
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,N_{c}}
are regular standard families, proving that
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,N_{c},c}
are also regular. As for
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,N_{c},u}
, for
\eta
small enough, we have that
\widetilde{\rho}^{(u)}_{j,p,\iota}\in{\mathcal{C}}(C\eta^{-1})\subseteq{\mathcal{C}}(\eta^{-2})
and since the decomposition (
6.54
) does not affect the boundary,
\mathcal{Z}(\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,N_{c},u})=\mathcal{Z}(\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,N_{c}})\leqslant B_{2}
. Finally, using that
\max\widetilde{\rho}^{(u)}_{j,p,\iota}/\min\widetilde{\rho}^{(u)}_{j,p,\iota}\leqslant 2e^{\varpi_{2}}
, we can apply Lemma
6.35
and
{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,N_{c},u}\in\mathfrak{R}_{\lfloor C\ln\eta^{-1}\rfloor}
. This concludes the proof of the Lemma.
∎

Recall that
{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}
are two
\eta
-coupled standard families. In the next result, we argue that if two standard families are close, the amount of mass that ends up in the hole for the two of them is also close.

Lemma 6.38
.

There exists
C>0
such that, for all
\eta>0
small enough and
t\in[0,t_{0}]
,

|\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{1}}({\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}})-\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{2}}({\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}})|\leqslant C\eta^{\frac{1}{3}}.

Proof.

Recall the notation
{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}=\{(p_{j},W_{j,\iota},\rho_{j})\}
. Then,

\begin{split}|\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{1}}({\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}})-&\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{2}}({\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}})|\leqslant\sum_{j}p_{j}\bigg|\int_{I_{W_{j,1}}}\rho_{j}(r){\mathbbm{1}}_{{\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}}}(r,\varphi_{W_{j,1}}(r))dr\\
&-\int_{I_{W_{j,2}}}\rho_{j}(r){\mathbbm{1}}_{{\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}}}(r,\varphi_{W_{j,2}}(r))dr\biggr|.\end{split}

Moreover, partitioning
W_{j,\iota}=\widehat{W}_{j,\iota}\cup(W_{j,\iota}\setminus\widehat{W}_{j,\iota})
in points which are linked by the fake holonomy and those that aren’t, we have

(6.55)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:split-hole-and-holonomy}\begin{split}&\int_{I_{W_{j,1}}}\rho_{j}(r){\mathbbm{1}}_{{\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}}}(r,\varphi_{W_{j,1}}(r))dr-\int_{I_{W_{j,2}}}\rho_{j}(r){\mathbbm{1}}_{{\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}}}(r,\varphi_{W_{j,2}}(r))dr\\
&=\int_{I_{\widehat{W}_{j,1}}}\rho_{j}(r){\mathbbm{1}}_{{\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}}}(r,\varphi_{W_{j,1}}(r))dr-\int_{I_{\widehat{W}_{j,2}}}\rho_{j}(r){\mathbbm{1}}_{{\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}}}(r,\varphi_{W_{j,2}}(r))dr\\
&+\int_{I_{W_{j,1}\setminus\widehat{W}_{j,1}}}\rho_{j}(r){\mathbbm{1}}_{{\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}}}(r,\varphi_{W_{j,1}}(r))dr-\int_{I_{W_{j,2}\setminus\widehat{W}_{j,2}}}\rho_{j}(r){\mathbbm{1}}_{{\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}}}(r,\varphi_{W_{j,2}}(r))dr.\end{split}

For any point
x\in\widehat{W}_{j,1}
, we have that
x
belongs to
{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}}
if and only if
\mathbb{h}_{N_{c}}(x)\in{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}}
. Indeed,
x
and
\mathbb{h}_{N_{c}}(x)
are connected via the curve
{\mathcal{H}}_{N_{c}}(x)\subset{\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{N_{c},t}^{{\mathbb{H}}}
. Hence, denoting by
\widehat{W}_{j,p,\iota}
the maximal connected components of
\widehat{W}_{j,\iota}
, we have that
{\mathbbm{1}}_{{\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}}}
is either one or zero on both
\widehat{W}_{j,p,1}
and
\widehat{W}_{j,p,2}
. Therefore, by (
6.51
), the contribution derived from the terms in the first line of the equation above is bounded by

\begin{split}&\sum_{j}p_{j}\sum_{p}\biggl|\int_{I_{\widehat{W}_{j,p,1}}}\rho_{j}-\int_{I_{\widehat{W}_{j,p,2}}}\rho_{j}\biggr|\leqslant C\eta^{\frac{1}{3}}.\end{split}

As for the contribution deriving from the second line of (
6.55
), we bound the difference with the sum of the two terms and, by Lemma
6.30
, we obtain

\sum_{j}p_{j}\int_{I_{W_{j,1}\setminus\widehat{W}_{j,1}}}\rho_{j}+\sum_{j}p_{j}\int_{I_{W_{j,2}\setminus\widehat{W}_{j,2}}}\rho_{j}\leqslant C\eta^{\frac{1}{2}}.

This concludes the proof of the Lemma.
∎

We conclude this section by summarizing all we need to know about the iterations of two coupled standard families. Here we are also more explicit on the orders of the quantifiers.

Lemma 6.39
.

There exist
C,\eta_{0},\overline{N}_{c}>0
, depending only on the billiard table and the initial regularity, such that, for all
N_{c}>\overline{N}_{c}
, there exists
t_{0}>0
, such that for all
t\in[0,t_{0}]
the following is true. For all
\eta\leqslant\eta_{0}
and any two
\eta
-coupled regular standard families
{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}
, there exist
\Omega_{c}^{\prime},\Omega_{u}^{\prime}\geqslant 0
,
\Omega_{c}^{\prime}+\Omega_{u}^{\prime}\leqslant 1
,
\Omega_{u}^{\prime}\leqslant\eta^{1/4}
, two
\chi\eta
-stacked regular standard families
{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,c}
and two standard families
{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,u}\in\mathfrak{R}_{\lfloor C\ln\eta^{-1}\rfloor}
such that, for all
\phi\in{\mathcal{C}}^{0}({\mathcal{M}})
,

\begin{split}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}}(\phi\circ\widehat{\mathcal{F}}^{N_{c}}_{t}{\mathbbm{1}}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}}})+\bigl(\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}}({\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}})-\min_{\iota\in\{1,2\}}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}}({\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}})\bigr)&({\mathcal{L}}_{t}^{N_{c}}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}})(\phi)\\
&=\Omega_{c}^{\prime}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,c}}(\phi)+\Omega_{u}^{\prime}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,u}}(\phi).\end{split}

Proof.

By (
6.53
) and Lemma
6.37
, there exist
\Omega_{c},\Omega_{u,\iota},b>0
and standard families such that

\begin{split}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}}(\phi\circ\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{N_{c}}{\mathbbm{1}}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}}})&=\Omega_{c}\mu_{\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,N_{c}}}(\phi)+\Omega_{u,\iota}\mu_{\overline{{\mathcal{G}}}_{\iota,N_{c}}}(\phi)\\
&=\Omega_{c}\bigl((1-b)\mu_{\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,N_{c},c}}(\phi)+b\mu_{\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,N_{c},u}}(\phi)\bigr)+\Omega_{u,\iota}\mu_{\overline{{\mathcal{G}}}_{\iota,N_{c}}}(\phi).\end{split}

The equation above yields the desired decomposition with

\Omega_{c}^{\prime}=\Omega_{c}(1-b)\quad\text{and}\quad\Omega_{u}^{\prime}=\Omega_{c}b+\Omega_{u,\iota}+\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}}({\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}})-\min_{\iota\in\{1,2\}}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}}({\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}}).

Accordingly,
{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,c}=\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,N_{c},c}
and
{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,u}
is a suitable convex combinations of
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,N_{c},u}
,
\overline{{\mathcal{G}}}_{\iota,N_{c}}
and the regular standard family
{\mathcal{L}}_{t}^{N_{c}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}
. Note that
\Omega_{c}^{\prime}+\Omega_{u}^{\prime}=1-\min_{\iota}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}}({\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}})
, so that
\Omega_{u}^{\prime}
does not depend on the family
\iota
. Moreover, by Lemmata
6.37
and
6.38
and Equation (
6.52
), for
\eta_{0}
small enough and
\eta\leqslant\eta_{0}
,

\Omega_{u}^{\prime}\leqslant b+\Omega_{u,\iota}+\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}}({\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}})-\min_{\iota\in\{1,2\}}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}}({\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}})\leqslant C\eta^{\frac{1}{3}}=C\eta_{0}^{\frac{1}{12}}\eta^{\frac{1}{4}}\leqslant\eta^{\frac{1}{4}},

as required by the statement. By Lemma
6.36

{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,c}=\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,N_{c},c}
is a regular standard family and by Lemmata
6.36
and
6.37

\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,N_{c},u}
and
\overline{{\mathcal{G}}}_{\iota,N_{c}}\in\mathfrak{R}_{\lfloor C\ln\eta^{-1}\rfloor}
. Moreover, by Proposition
4.4
about invariance of standard families,
{\mathcal{L}}_{t}^{N_{c}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}
are regular standard families and so they belong to
\mathfrak{R}_{\lfloor C\ln\eta^{-1}\rfloor}
.
Since a convex combination of standard families with a certain regularity has the same regularity, we have that
{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,u}\in\mathfrak{R}_{\lfloor C\ln\eta^{-1}\rfloor}
.
∎

6.8.
Decay of Correlations

In this section we introduce a linear scheme that records the evolution in time of how much mass is coupled, at which distance, and the regularity of the uncoupled part. We use this to prove Theorem
4.7
about decay of correlations.

Let
\eta_{0}>0
,
\eta_{0}\leqslant 10^{-10}
be small enough so that Lemma
6.39
holds. We let
N_{c}
be big enough such that Lemma
6.39
and Lemma
6.23
hold for the given
\eta_{0}
. Recall that
\chi\in(0,1/2)
is introduced before Lemma
6.24
.

Definition 6.40

(Coupled-uncoupled decomposition)
.

We say that two regular standard families
{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}
admit a
(c_{r},u_{r})
-decomposition for
r\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}
,
c_{r},u_{r},\in[0,1]
, if there exist
\chi^{r}\eta_{0}
-coupled regular standard families
{\mathcal{G}}_{1,r}^{c}
,
{\mathcal{G}}_{2,r}^{c}
and standard families
{\mathcal{G}}_{1,r}^{u}
,
{\mathcal{G}}_{2,r}^{u}
in
\mathfrak{R}_{r}
such that, for
\iota\in\{1,2\}
,

\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}}=\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}u_{r}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{u}}+\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}c_{r}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{c}}.

Recall Definition
6.34
for the regularity classes
\mathfrak{R}_{r}
. We are now ready to implement the coupling scheme for the leaky system. Using the temporary notation

X(\phi)=\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}u_{r}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{u}}(\phi\circ{\mathcal{F}}^{N_{c}}{\mathbbm{1}}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}}})+\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}c_{r}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{c}}(\phi\circ{\mathcal{F}}^{N_{c}}{\mathbbm{1}}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}}}),

we have

(6.56)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:support1}\begin{split}({\mathcal{L}}^{N_{c}}_{t}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}})(\phi)&=\frac{X(\phi)}{\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}}({\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}})}=X(\phi)+X(\phi)\biggl(\frac{1}{\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}}({\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}})}-1\biggr)\\
&=X(\phi)+({\mathcal{L}}_{t}^{N_{c}}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}})(\phi)(1-\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}}({\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}})).\end{split}

If the standard families
{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}
admit a decomposition as in Definition
6.40
, we can write

1-\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}}({\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}})=\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}}({\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}})=\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}u_{r}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{u}}({\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}})+\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}c_{r}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{r}}({\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}}).

Inserting the definition of
X(\phi)
and the expression above in (
6.56
), we obtain

\begin{split}({\mathcal{L}}^{N_{c}}_{t}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}})(\phi)&=\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}u_{r}\bigl(\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{u}}(\phi\circ{\mathcal{F}}^{N_{c}}{\mathbbm{1}}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}}})+\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{u}}({\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}})({\mathcal{L}}_{t}^{N_{c}}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}})(\phi)\bigr)\\
&+\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}c_{r}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{c}}(\phi\circ{\mathcal{F}}^{N_{c}}{\mathbbm{1}}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}}})+({\mathcal{L}}_{t}^{N_{c}}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}})(\phi)\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}c_{r}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{c}}({\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}}),\end{split}

and, denoting by

A_{\iota,r}=\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{c}}({\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}})-\min_{\iota\in\{1,2\}}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{c}}({\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}}),

we obtain

\begin{split}({\mathcal{L}}^{N_{c}}_{t}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}})(\phi)&=\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}u_{r}\bigl(\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{u}}(\phi\circ{\mathcal{F}}^{N_{c}}{\mathbbm{1}}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}}})+\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{u}}({\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}})({\mathcal{L}}_{t}^{N_{c}}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}})(\phi)\bigr)\\
&+\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}c_{r}\bigl(\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{c}}(\phi\circ{\mathcal{F}}^{N_{c}}{\mathbbm{1}}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}}})+A_{\iota,r}({\mathcal{L}}_{t}^{N_{c}}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}})(\phi)\bigr)\\
&+({\mathcal{L}}_{t}^{N_{c}}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}})(\phi)\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}c_{r}\min_{\iota\in\{1,2\}}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{c}}({\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}}).\end{split}

After an algebraic manipulation, we obtain the following expression for
({\mathcal{L}}_{t}^{N_{c}}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}})(\phi)
,

(6.57)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:very-funny-decomposition}\begin{split}({\mathcal{L}}^{N_{c}}_{t}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}})(\phi)&=\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\frac{u_{r}}{1-\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}c_{r}\min_{\iota\in\{1,2\}}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{c}}({\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}})}\bigl(\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{u}}(\phi\circ{\mathcal{F}}^{N_{c}}{\mathbbm{1}}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}}})\\[10.0pt]
&\hskip 142.26378pt+\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{u}}({\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}})({\mathcal{L}}_{t}^{N_{c}}\mu_{\iota})(\phi)\bigr)\\[8.0pt]
&\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!+\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\frac{c_{r}}{1-\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}c_{r}\min_{\iota\in\{1,2\}}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{c}}({\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}})}\bigl(\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{c}}(\phi\circ{\mathcal{F}}^{N_{c}}{\mathbbm{1}}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}}})+A_{\iota,r}({\mathcal{L}}_{t}^{N_{c}}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}})(\phi)\bigr).\end{split}

Note that the above expression contains
({\mathcal{L}}^{N_{c}}_{t}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}})(\phi)
also on the right hand side. The reason for this decomposition is the following. After conditioning on an irregular standard family (i.e., on
{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{u}
with
r\geqslant 1
), we would like to observe a regularizing effect of the dynamics. However, in the presence of the hole, small pieces may be cut so that the surviving part is irremediably small, posing some problem. To obtain regularization we may argue in the following manner: if some mass enters the hole, it is as if an equivalent mass is generated in the form of the whole push-forward
{\mathcal{L}}_{t}^{N_{c}}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}}
. Equation (
6.57
) makes this fact clear. By Proposition
4.4
on invariance of standard families, which we already established, the measures
{\mathcal{L}}_{t}^{N_{c}}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}}
correspond to regular standard families. Therefore, when some mass enters the hole, it behaves as if it is automatically regularized. The net effect is that short standard families recover. We express this in the following Lemma.

Lemma 6.41
.

There exists
t_{0}>0
small enough such, that for all
t\in[0,t_{0}]
and
r\geqslant 1
we have

\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{u}}(\phi\circ{\mathcal{F}}^{N_{c}}{\mathbbm{1}}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}}})+\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{u}}({\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}})({\mathcal{L}}_{t}^{N_{c}}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}})(\phi)=\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r-1}^{u}}(\phi),

for some standard family
{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r-1}^{u}\in\mathfrak{R}_{r-1}
.

Proof.

Denote by
{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{u}=\{(p_{j,\iota},W_{j,\iota},\rho_{j,\iota})\}
and let
W_{j,k,\iota}
be the maximal connected subsets of
W_{j,\iota}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{N_{c},t}^{{\mathbb{H}}}
. Set also
\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{N_{c}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{u}=\{(p_{j,k,\iota},\widehat{{\mathcal{F}}}_{t}^{N_{c}}(W_{j,k,\iota}),\rho_{j,k,\iota})\}
and let
{\mathcal{A}}_{\iota}
be the collection of indices
(j,k)
such that
W_{j,k,\iota}\subset{\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}}
. We have

(6.58)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:mass-enter-hole}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{u}}({\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}})=\sum_{(j,k)\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\iota}}p_{j,k,\iota}.

By Proposition
4.4
about invariance of standard families,
{\mathcal{L}}_{t}^{N_{c}}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}}=\mu_{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}}
for some regular standard families
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}=\{(\widetilde{p}_{s,q,\iota},\widetilde{\ell}_{s,q,\iota})\}
. We set

{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r-1}^{u}=\{(p_{j,k,\iota},\widehat{{\mathcal{F}}}_{t}^{N_{c}}(W_{j,k,\iota}),\rho_{j,k,\iota})\}_{(j,k)\notin{\mathcal{A}}_{\iota}}\cup\{(\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{u}}({\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}})\widetilde{p}_{s,q,\iota},\widetilde{\ell}_{s,q,\iota})\}.

The standard families
{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r-1}^{u}
yield the decomposition in the statement of the Lemma but to conclude the proof we need to bound the regularity of
{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r-1}^{u}
. Note that the densities supported by
{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r-1}^{u}
either belong to
{\mathcal{C}}(\varpi_{2})
and are already regular, or they are push-forwards of a density supported on an unstable curve in
{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{u}
. By definition, these densities become regular after at most
r
iterates of
\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{N_{c}}
, and so the densities supported by
{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r-1}^{u}
become regular after at most
r-1
iterates of
\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{N_{c}}
. As for the boundary, by the equation above,

\mathcal{Z}({\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r-1}^{u})=\sum_{(j,k)\notin{\mathcal{A}}_{\iota}}p_{j,k,\iota}\frac{1}{|\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{N_{c}}(W_{j,k,\iota})|}+\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{u}}({\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}})\mathcal{Z}(\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}).

By (
6.58
) and the fact that
\mathcal{Z}(\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota})\leqslant B_{2}
because
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}
are regular standard families,

(6.59)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:support-recovery-hole}\mathcal{Z}({\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r-1}^{u})\leqslant\sum_{(j,k)\notin{\mathcal{A}}_{\iota}}p_{j,k,\iota}\frac{1}{|\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{N_{c}}(W_{j,k,\iota})|}+B_{2}\sum_{(j,k)\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\iota}}p_{j,k,\iota}.

Consider now an unstable curve
W
such that
W\subset{\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}}=\cup_{n=0}^{N_{c}}{\mathcal{F}}^{-n}({\mathcal{H}}_{t})
. Then, by Lemma
5.11
(which is independent on the results of this section),
W\subset\bigl[{\mathcal{S}}_{N_{c}}\cup{\mathcal{F}}^{-N_{c}}(\partial{\mathcal{H}}_{t})\bigr]_{h_{N_{c}}(t)}
for some function
h_{N_{c}}
such that
\lim_{t\to 0}h_{N_{c}}(t)=0
. By transverslity, this implies that
|W|\leqslant C_{\mathrm{cone}}h_{N_{c}}(t)
. In particular, using any bound on the expansion (e.g., iterating Lemma
3.1
), there exists
t_{0}>0
small enough such that
1/|\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{N_{c}}(W)|\geqslant B_{2}
for any
W\subset{\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}}
and
t\in[0,t_{0}]
. Applying this estimate to the unstable curves
W_{j,k,\iota}\subset{\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}}
with
(j,k)\in{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
and by (
6.59
), we have

\mathcal{Z}({\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r-1}^{u})\leqslant\sum_{(j,k)\notin{\mathcal{A}}_{\iota}}p_{j,k,\iota}\frac{1}{|\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{N_{c}}(W_{j,k,\iota})|}+\sum_{(j,k)\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\iota}}p_{j,k,\iota}\frac{1}{|\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{N_{c}}(W_{j,k,\iota})|}=\mathcal{Z}(\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{N_{c}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{u}).

Since
{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{u}\in\mathfrak{R}_{r}
and consequently
\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{N_{c}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{u}\in\mathfrak{R}_{r-1}
, we have that
{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r-1}^{u}\in\mathfrak{R}_{r-1}
as well. This concludes the proof of the statement.
∎

The following linear scheme encodes all the results obtained so far on coupling. We use the notation
M{\mathbb{N}}=\{M,2M,...\}
.

Lemma 6.42
.

Let
{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}
be two regular standard families admitting a
(c_{r},u_{r})
-decomposition as in Definition
6.40
. There exist
C,p_{c}>0
,
M\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
t_{0}>0
such that, for all
t\in[0,t_{0}]
, the standard families
{\mathcal{L}}_{t}^{N_{c}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}
admit a
(c_{r}^{\prime},u_{r}^{\prime})
-decomposition with

\begin{split}&c_{r}^{\prime}\leqslant(1+Ct)c_{r-1}\quad\text{if }r\geqslant 1,\\
&c_{0}^{\prime}\leqslant(1+Ct)p_{c}u_{0},\\
&u_{0}^{\prime}\leqslant(1+Ct)(u_{1}+(1-p_{c})u_{0}),\\
&u_{r}^{\prime}\leqslant(1+Ct)\begin{cases}u_{r+1}&\text{if }r\notin M{\mathbb{N}}\\
u_{r+1}+\eta_{0}^{\frac{1}{4}}c_{\frac{r}{M}-1}\chi^{\frac{1}{4}(\frac{r}{M}-1)}&\text{if }r\in M{\mathbb{N}}.\end{cases}\end{split}

Proof.

We decompose
