{\mathcal{L}}_{t}^{N_{c}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}
as in (
6.57
) and we study each term separately. For convenience, we set

W_{t}=\frac{1}{1-\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}c_{r}\min_{\iota\in\{1,2\}}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{c}}({\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}})}.

By Lemma
6.41
, for
t_{0}>0
small enough, there exist standard families
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r-1}^{u}\in\mathfrak{R}_{r-1}
such that, for any
t\in[0,t_{0}]
and
\phi\in{\mathcal{C}}^{0}({\mathcal{M}})
,

\begin{split}\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}u_{r}\bigl(\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{u}}(\phi\circ{\mathcal{F}}^{N_{c}}{\mathbbm{1}}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}}})+\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{u}}({\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}})({\mathcal{L}}_{t}^{N_{c}}\mu_{\iota})(\phi)\bigr)&=\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}u_{r}\mu_{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r-1}^{u}}(\phi).\end{split}

Moreover, by Lemma
6.23
and our choice of
N_{c}
, for
t_{0}>0
small enough, there exist
p_{c},p_{\iota,u}>0
, two
\eta_{0}
-coupled regular standard pairs
\ell_{\iota,c}
and two regular standard families
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,0}^{u}
such that

\begin{split}&u_{0}\bigl(\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,0}^{u}}(\phi\circ{\mathcal{F}}^{N_{c}}{\mathbbm{1}}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}}})+\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,0}^{u}}({\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}})({\mathcal{L}}_{t}^{N_{c}}\mu_{\iota})(\phi)\bigr)\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt=u_{0}\bigl(p_{c}\mu_{\ell_{\iota,c}}(\phi)+p_{\iota,u}\mu_{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,0}^{u}}(\phi)+\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,0}^{u}}({\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}})({\mathcal{L}}_{t}^{N_{c}}\mu_{\iota})(\phi)\bigr).\end{split}

By the invariance established in Proposition
4.4
,
{\mathcal{L}}_{t}^{N_{c}}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}}
are associated to regular standard families. Hence, there exist yet other two regular standard families, which we also denote by
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,0}^{u}
, such that,

u_{0}\bigl(\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,0}^{u}}(\phi\circ{\mathcal{F}}^{N_{c}}{\mathbbm{1}}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}}})+\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,0}^{u}}({\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}})({\mathcal{L}}_{t}^{N_{c}}\mu_{\iota})(\phi)\bigr)=u_{0}\bigl(p_{c}\mu_{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,0}^{c}}(\phi)+(1-p_{c})\mu_{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,0}^{u}}(\phi)\bigr).

In the last equality we used the notation
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,0}^{c}=\ell_{\iota,c}
and the fact that the total mass on the l.h.s.​ has to coincide with the total mass on the r.h.s.​ Continuing with the last terms, by Lemma
6.39
, there exists
C>0
and
t_{0}>0
such that, for each
r\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
t\in[0,t_{0}]
, there exist
\Omega_{c,r}^{\prime},\Omega_{u,r}^{\prime}\in[0,1]
,
\Omega_{u,r}^{\prime}\leqslant\eta_{0}^{1/4}\chi^{r/4}
, and
\chi^{r+1}\eta_{0}
-coupled regular standard families
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r+1}^{c}
and standard families
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,M(r+1)}^{u}\in\mathfrak{R}_{C\ln\eta_{0}^{-1}\chi^{-r}}
such that

(6.60)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:tiring}\begin{split}\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}c_{r}\bigl(\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{c}}(\phi\circ{\mathcal{F}}^{N_{c}}{\mathbbm{1}}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}}})&+A_{\iota,r}({\mathcal{L}}_{t}^{N_{c}}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}})(\phi)\bigr)\\
&=\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}c_{r}\bigl(\Omega_{c,r}^{\prime}\mu_{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r+1}^{c}}(\phi)+\Omega_{u,r}^{\prime}\mu_{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,M(r+1)}^{u}}(\phi)\bigr).\end{split}

Note that
\mathfrak{R}_{C\ln\eta_{0}^{-1}\chi^{-r}}\subset\mathfrak{R}_{M(r+1)}
for
M\in{\mathbb{N}}
large enough, so that
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,M(r+1)}^{u}\in\mathfrak{R}_{M(r+1)}
, explaining the notation. Therefore, we can re-write the expression (
6.57
) as

\begin{split}({\mathcal{L}}^{N_{c}}_{t}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}})&=W_{t}\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}u_{r}\mu_{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r-1}^{u}}+W_{t}u_{0}\bigl(p_{c}\mu_{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,0}^{c}}+(1-p_{c})\mu_{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,0}^{u}}\bigr)\\
&+W_{t}\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}c_{r}\bigl(\Omega_{c,r}^{\prime}\mu_{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r+1}^{c}}+\Omega_{u,r}^{\prime}\mu_{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,M(r+1)}^{u}}\bigr).\end{split}

Relabeling the indices, we obtain,

\begin{split}({\mathcal{L}}^{N_{c}}_{t}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}})&=W_{t}\mu_{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,0}^{u}}\bigl((1-p_{c})u_{0}+u_{1}\bigr)+W_{t}\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}u_{r+1}\mu_{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{u}}+W_{t}u_{0}p_{c}\mu_{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,0}^{c}}\\
&+W_{t}\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}c_{r-1}\Omega_{c,r-1}^{\prime}\mu_{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{c}}(\phi)+W_{t}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r=M\\
r\equiv 0\ (\mathrm{mod}\ M)\end{subarray}}^{\infty}c_{\frac{r}{M}-1}\Omega_{u,\frac{r}{M}-1}^{\prime}\mu_{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{u}}\quad\text{and}\\
&\hskip 85.35826pt\Omega_{u,\frac{r}{M}-1}^{\prime}\leqslant\eta_{0}^{\frac{1}{4}}\chi^{\frac{1}{4}(\frac{r}{M}-1)}.\end{split}

Once we take care of the common factor
W_{t}
, we can compare the expression above with Definition
6.40
and obtain the desired decomposition. By Definition
6.40
,
{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{c}
are regular standard families. Therefore, by Lemma
6.16
, there exists
C_{N_{c}}>0
such that
\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{c}}({\mathcal{M}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{t}^{N_{c}})\leqslant C_{N_{c}}t
for both standard families. Therefore, for
t_{0}>0
small enough and because
\sum_{r}c_{r}
is less than one

|W_{t}|\leqslant\frac{1}{1-C_{N_{c}}\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}c_{r}t}\leqslant\frac{1}{1-C_{N_{c}}t}\leqslant 1+2C_{N_{c}}t.

Here we can eliminate the subscript in
C_{N_{c}}
since
N_{c}
is already fixed depending on
\eta_{0}
, which in turn depends on the table and the initial regularity.
This concludes the proof of the Lemma after renaming the constant.
∎

In order to prove decay of correlations with the scheme introduced above, we introduce the following norm. Recall
M\in{\mathbb{N}}
from the statement of Lemma
6.42
. Let
\psi_{-}<1
and
\psi_{+}>1
such that

\chi^{1/4}<(1/2)^{1/4}<\psi_{-}<9/10,\qquad 1<\psi_{+}<\biggl(\frac{\psi_{-}}{\chi^{1/4}}\biggr)^{\frac{1}{M}}\quad\text{and}\quad\psi_{+}^{M+1}\eta_{0}^{\frac{1}{4}}<\frac{1}{100}.

The above relations hold choosing
\psi_{+}
sufficiently close to
1
. For
c_{r},u_{r}\in[0,1]
, set

(6.61)

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:fancy-norm}\|(c_{r},u_{r})\|_{*}=\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}u_{r}\psi_{+}^{r+1}+\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}c_{r}\psi_{-}^{r}.

Lemma 6.43
.

Let
{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}
be two regular standard families admitting a
(c_{r},u_{r})
-decomposition as in Definition
6.40
. There exist
\tau\in(0,1)
and
t_{0}>0
such that, for all
t\in[0,t_{0}]
, the standard families
{\mathcal{L}}_{t}^{N_{c}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}
admit a
(c_{r}^{\prime},u_{r}^{\prime})
-decomposition such that

\|(c_{r}^{\prime},u_{r}^{\prime})\|_{*}\leqslant\tau\|(c_{r},u_{r})\|_{*}.

Proof.

By Lemma
6.42
,
{\mathcal{L}}_{t}^{N_{c}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}
admit a
(c_{r}^{\prime},u_{r}^{\prime})
-decomposition and, for some
p_{c}>0
,

\begin{split}&\|(c_{r}^{\prime},u_{r}^{\prime})\|_{*}=\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\psi_{+}^{r+1}u_{r}^{\prime}+\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\psi_{-}^{r}c_{r}^{\prime}\\
&\leqslant(1+Ct)\biggl(\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\psi_{+}^{r+1}u_{r+1}+\eta_{0}^{\frac{1}{4}}\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\psi_{+}^{(r+1)M+1}\chi^{\frac{r}{4}}c_{r}+\psi_{+}(u_{1}+(1-p_{c})u_{0})+p_{c}u_{0}+\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\psi_{-}^{r}c_{r-1}\biggr).\end{split}

After renaming the indices, we obtain for the part in big brackets

\begin{split}\psi_{+}^{-1}\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}&\psi_{+}^{r+1}u_{r}+\psi_{+}u_{0}((1-p_{c})+\psi_{+}^{-1}p_{c})+\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\psi_{-}^{r}c_{r}(\psi_{-}+\eta_{0}^{\frac{1}{4}}\psi_{+}^{(r+1)M+1}\psi_{-}^{-r}\chi^{\frac{r}{4}})\leqslant\tau\|(c_{r},u_{r})\|_{*},\end{split}

where we have set

\tau=\max\biggl\{(1-p_{c})+\psi_{+}^{-1}p_{c},\text{ }\sup_{r\geqslant 0}\bigl\{\psi_{-}+\eta_{0}^{\frac{1}{4}}\psi_{+}^{(r+1)M+1}\psi_{-}^{-r}\chi^{\frac{r}{4}}\bigr\}\biggr\}.

By the conditions before (
6.61
), we have that
\psi_{+}^{M}\psi_{-}^{-1}\chi^{1/4}<1
and consequently, since
\eta_{0}^{1/4}\psi_{+}^{M+1}<1/100
, we have that
\eta_{0}^{1/4}\psi_{+}^{M(r+1)+1}\psi_{-}^{-r}\chi^{r/4}\leqslant\eta_{0}^{1/4}\psi_{+}^{M+1}<1/100
for all
r\in{\mathbb{N}}
. Therefore, using also that
\psi_{-}<9/10
and that
p_{c}
is strictly positive,

\tau\leqslant\max\{(1-p_{c})+\psi_{+}^{-1}p_{c},\psi_{-}+\eta_{0}^{\frac{1}{4}}\psi_{+}\}<1.

Thus, we have obtained that
\|(c_{r}^{\prime},u_{r}^{\prime})\|_{*}\leqslant(1+Ct)\tau\|(c_{r},u_{r})\|_{*}
and the Lemma follows by taking
t_{0}
small enough and renaming
\tau
.
∎

We now show how the above implies exponential decay of correlations.

Proof of Theorem
4.7
.

Let
{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}
be two initial regular standard families and
t_{0}
be small enough so that Lemma
6.43
applies. For any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, write
n=pN_{c}+n_{0}
with
n_{0}<N_{c}
. By Proposition
4.4
, the measures
{\mathcal{L}}_{t}^{n_{0}}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}}
are associated to the regular standard families
{\mathcal{L}}_{t}^{n_{0}}{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}
. Therefore, they admit a
(c_{r},u_{r})
-decomposition with
u_{0}=1
and
u_{r}=c_{r}=c_{0}=0
for
r
positive. Applying repeatedly Lemma
6.42
, we obtain that for any
p\in{\mathbb{N}}
the measures
{\mathcal{L}}_{t}^{pN_{c}}({\mathcal{L}}_{t}^{n_{0}}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota}})
admit a
(c_{r},u_{r})
-decomposition. Moreover, denoting these decompositions by
(c_{r}(p),u_{r}(p))
, we have, for any
\phi\in{\mathcal{C}}^{1}({\mathcal{M}})
,

|{\mathcal{L}}_{t}^{n}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{1}}(\phi)-{\mathcal{L}}_{t}^{n}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{2}}(\phi)|\leqslant\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}u_{r}(p)|\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{1,r}^{u}}(\phi)-\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{2,r}^{u}}(\phi)|+\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}c_{r}(p)|\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{1,r}^{c}}(\phi)-\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{2,r}^{c}}(\phi)|.

Since
{\mathcal{G}}_{\iota,r}^{c}
are
\chi^{r}\eta_{0}
-coupled, the quantity above is bounded by

2\|\phi\|_{{\mathcal{C}}^{0}}\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}u_{r}(p)+\eta_{0}\|\phi\|_{{\mathcal{C}}^{1}}\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}c_{r}(p)\chi^{r}\leqslant 2\|\phi\|_{{\mathcal{C}}^{1}}\|(c_{r}(p),u_{r}(p))\|_{*},

where in the inequality we used that
\chi\leqslant\chi^{1/4}\leqslant\psi_{-}
and
\psi_{+}\geqslant 1
. Therefore, using Lemma
6.43
repeatedly and recalling that
p=\lfloor n/N_{c}\rfloor
, we have, for some
\tau\in(0,1)
,

|{\mathcal{L}}_{t}^{n}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{1}}(\phi)-{\mathcal{L}}_{t}^{n}\mu_{{\mathcal{G}}_{2}}(\phi)|\leqslant 2\|\phi\|_{{\mathcal{C}}^{1}}\tau^{p}\|(c_{r}(0),u_{r}(0))\|_{*}=2\psi_{+}\|\phi\|_{{\mathcal{C}}^{1}}\tau^{\lfloor\frac{n}{N}_{c}\rfloor},

where in the last equality we have used that
c_{r}(0)=0
,
u_{0}(0)=1
and
u_{r}(0)=0
for
r\geqslant 1
. By setting
\gamma=\tau^{1/N_{c}}
in the equation above, we conclude the proof of the theorem.
∎
